  // Escape closes the drawer — expected of anything overlaying the page, and
  // the only exit for a keyboard user who never reaches the scrim.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!open) return;

    function onKeyDown(event: KeyboardEvent) {
      if (event.key === "Escape") setOpen(false);
    }

    document.addEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
    return () => document.removeEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
  }, [open]);

  // Lock the page behind the drawer. Without this the body scrolls under the
  // scrim on iOS and the user loses their place in the list they came from.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!open) return;

    const previous = document.body.style.overflow;
    document.body.style.overflow = "hidden";
    return () => {
      document.body.style.overflow = previous;
    };
  }, [open]);

  const value = useMemo<NavState>(
    () => ({ open, toggle, close, expanded, toggleExpanded }),
    [open, toggle, close, expanded, toggleExpanded],
  );

  return <NavContext.Provider value={value}>{children}</NavContext.Provider>;
}

export function useNav(): NavState {
  const context = useContext(NavContext);

  if (!context) {
    throw new Error("useNav must be used inside <NavProvider>.");
  }

  return context;
}

===== src/components/layout/stub-action-bridge.tsx =====
"use client";

import { useEffect } from "react";
import { usePathname } from "next/navigation";

import { toast } from "@/lib/toast";

/**
 * Transitional scaffold: makes unwired controls admit that they are unwired.
 *
 * ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 * WHY THIS EXISTS
 *
 * Most screens in this app still render placeholder data. That left ~270
 * buttons and `href="#"` links that look live, are clicked, and do nothing —
 * which users read as "the product is broken", not "this part isn't built".
 *
 * Rather than hand-editing every one of those call sites (and getting some
 * wrong), a single delegated listener reports any click that no real handler
 * claimed.
 *
 * THE CONTRACT — read this before adding a working button
 *
 * A control is treated as LIVE, and left alone, when any of these hold:
 *   • it (or an ancestor) has `data-live`  ← <Button> sets this for you
 *   • it is a submit/reset button, or sits inside a <form>
 *   • it is an <a> with a real href (not "#", not empty)
 *   • it is inside [data-live-region] — use this to exempt a whole subtree
 *
 * So: build interactive controls with <Button>/<SubmitButton> from
 * `components/ui/button` and this never fires for them. If you hand-roll a
 * <button onClick={...}>, add `data-live` yourself.
 *
 * DELETE THIS once the screens are wired. It is scaffolding, not architecture —
 * its whole job is to be unnecessary.
 * ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 */
export function StubActionBridge() {
  const pathname = usePathname();

  useEffect(() => {
    function onClick(event: MouseEvent) {
      // Let modified clicks through — the user is opening a tab, not acting.
      if (event.defaultPrevented || event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey || event.shiftKey) {
        return;
      }

      const target = event.target as Element | null;
      const control = target?.closest?.("button, a, [role='button']");
      if (!(control instanceof HTMLElement)) return;

      if (isLive(control)) return;

      // An <a href="#"> would otherwise jump the scroll position to the top,
      // which on a long table looks like the page reset itself.
      if (control instanceof HTMLAnchorElement) event.preventDefault();

      toast.notImplemented(labelFor(control));
    }

    document.addEventListener("click", onClick);
    return () => document.removeEventListener("click", onClick);
    // Re-bound per route only so the listener never outlives a navigation in
    // a way that leaks across route-level remounts.
  }, [pathname]);

  return null;
}

function isLive(el: HTMLElement): boolean {
  if (el.closest("[data-live], [data-live-region]")) return true;

  // Anything that submits a form is doing real work — logout is the live
  // example today.
  if (el instanceof HTMLButtonElement) {
    if (el.type === "submit" || el.type === "reset") return true;
    if (el.form) return true;
  }

  if (el instanceof HTMLAnchorElement) {
    const href = el.getAttribute("href");
    if (href && href !== "#" && !href.startsWith("#")) return true;
  }

  // Labels drive their own control (file pickers, checkboxes, the search box).
  if (el.closest("label")) return true;

  return false;
}

/** Human label for the toast: visible text, then aria-label, then title. */
function labelFor(el: HTMLElement): string {
  const text = el.textContent?.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
  if (text) return truncate(text);

  const aria = el.getAttribute("aria-label") ?? el.getAttribute("title");
  if (aria) return truncate(aria.trim());

  return "This action";
}

function truncate(value: string): string {
  return value.length > 40 ? `${value.slice(0, 39)}…` : value;
}

===== src/components/layout/topbar.tsx =====
"use client";

import { usePathname } from "next/navigation";
import { useState } from "react";

import { ROLE_LABEL } from "@/config/roles";
import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";
import { useNav } from "@/components/layout/nav-context";
import { initials } from "@/lib/format";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import type { CurrentUser } from "@/types";

/**
 * Top bar.
 *
 * Client Component because it owns the mobile rail toggle. The user projection
 * it receives comes from the DAL and never contains `accessToken` — see
 * `getCurrentUser()`.
 *
 * Stitch ships two variants of this bar: the dashboard leads with a greeting,
 * the list screens lead with a search field. Same bar, so the variant is
 * derived from the route rather than duplicated into two components. It has to
 * be derived here — a layout cannot see which page it is wrapping.
 *
 * Below `sm` the search field collapses to an icon that opens a full-width row
 * underneath. At 360px the bar cannot hold a hamburger, a greeting, a search
 * box and three controls at once, and shrinking the input until it fits makes
 * it useless for a query longer than one word.
 */
export function Topbar({
  user,
  searchPlaceholder = "Search...",
  unreadNotifications = false,
}: {
  user: CurrentUser;
  searchPlaceholder?: string;
  /** Renders the dot on the bell. Wire to `notifications` once the API lands. */
  unreadNotifications?: boolean;
}) {
  const { toggle } = useNav();
  const pathname = usePathname();
  const [searchOpen, setSearchOpen] = useState(false);

  const greeting = pathname.endsWith("/dashboard");
  const label = user.displayName ?? user.username;
  const firstName = label.split(/\s+/)[0];

  return (
    <header className="sticky top-0 z-30 w-full shrink-0 border-b border-outline-variant/20 bg-surface-bright/80 backdrop-blur-md">
      <div className="flex h-16 w-full items-center justify-between gap-2 px-4 sm:h-20 sm:gap-4 sm:px-6 lg:px-container-margin">
        <div className="flex min-w-0 flex-1 items-center gap-2 sm:gap-4">
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={toggle}
            data-live=""
            aria-label="Open navigation"
            className="-ml-2 flex size-10 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full text-on-surface-variant transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-primary/40 md:hidden"
          >
            <Icon name="menu" />
          </button>

          {greeting ? (
            <div className="flex min-w-0 flex-col">
              <div className="flex min-w-0 items-center gap-2 sm:gap-3">
                <h1 className="truncate font-display-greeting text-base text-primary sm:text-display-greeting">
                  Hello {firstName}
                </h1>
                {/* Which role the session is acting as. Nine roles share this
                    shell and several users hold more than one account, so the
                    active one is worth stating rather than inferring from the
                    rail. */}
                <span className="hidden shrink-0 rounded-full border border-primary-container/20 bg-blue-soft px-2.5 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-bold text-primary-container sm:inline">
                  {ROLE_LABEL[user.role]}
                </span>
              </div>
              <p className="hidden truncate font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant sm:block">
                Welcome back. Here&rsquo;s your organisation today.
              </p>
            </div>
          ) : (
            <SearchField
              placeholder={searchPlaceholder}
              className="hidden w-full max-w-xs sm:flex lg:max-w-sm"
            />
          )}
        </div>

        <div className="flex shrink-0 items-center gap-1 sm:gap-3 lg:gap-4">
          {greeting ? (
            <SearchField
              placeholder={searchPlaceholder}
              className="hidden w-64 lg:flex"
            />
          ) : null}

          {/* Small screens get the search as a disclosure rather than a
              permanently cramped input. */}
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() => setSearchOpen((v) => !v)}
            data-live=""
            aria-label="Search"
            aria-expanded={searchOpen}
            className={cn(
              "flex size-10 items-center justify-center rounded-full text-on-surface-variant transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-primary/40",
              greeting ? "lg:hidden" : "sm:hidden",
            )}
          >
            <Icon name={searchOpen ? "close" : "search"} />
          </button>

          <button
            type="button"
            aria-label="Notifications"
            className="relative flex size-10 items-center justify-center rounded-full text-on-surface-variant transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-primary/40"
          >
            <Icon name="notifications" />
            {unreadNotifications ? (
              <span
                aria-hidden
                className="absolute right-2 top-2 size-2 rounded-full bg-error"
              />
            ) : null}
          </button>

          <button
            type="button"
            aria-label="Messages"
            className="hidden size-10 items-center justify-center rounded-full text-on-surface-variant transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-primary/40 sm:flex"
          >
            <Icon name="mail" />
          </button>

          {/* Profile. Initials rather than a photo — `employees.passport_photo_url`
              is not on the session, and the rail already owns sign-out. */}
          <div className="flex items-center gap-3 border-outline-variant/30 sm:border-l sm:pl-4">
            <div className="hidden text-right lg:block">
              <p className="max-w-[16ch] truncate font-body-md text-body-md font-semibold text-on-surface">
                {label}
              </p>
              <p className="text-[10px] text-on-surface-variant">
                {ROLE_LABEL[user.role]}
              </p>
            </div>
            <span
              aria-hidden
              className="flex size-9 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-primary-container text-[11px] font-bold text-on-primary sm:size-10"
            >
              {initials(label)}
            </span>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>

      {/* Disclosure row — only rendered at the widths where the inline field
          is hidden, so there is never a second search box on screen. */}
      {searchOpen ? (
        <div
          className={cn(
            "border-t border-outline-variant/20 px-4 py-3 sm:px-6",
            greeting ? "lg:hidden" : "sm:hidden",
          )}
        >
          <SearchField placeholder={searchPlaceholder} autoFocus className="flex w-full" />
        </div>
      ) : null}
    </header>
  );
}

function SearchField({
  placeholder,
  className,
  autoFocus,
}: {
  placeholder: string;
  className?: string;
  autoFocus?: boolean;
}) {
  return (
    <label
      className={cn(
        "items-center gap-2 rounded-full border border-outline-variant bg-surface-container-lowest px-4 py-2 transition-all focus-within:border-primary-container focus-within:ring-2 focus-within:ring-primary-container/20",
        className ?? "flex",
      )}
    >
      <Icon name="search" className="shrink-0 text-lg text-outline" />
      <span className="sr-only">{placeholder}</span>
      <input
        type="search"
        placeholder={placeholder}
        // Focus follows the disclosure the user just opened; only ever set
        // when they activated the search toggle themselves.
        autoFocus={autoFocus}
        className="w-full min-w-0 border-none bg-transparent p-0 font-body-md text-body-md outline-none placeholder:text-outline focus:ring-0"
      />
    </label>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/alert-spotlight.tsx =====
import Link from "next/link";

import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Navy spotlight tile for a single high-priority system message.
 *
 * One message, not a list — it competes for attention with the whole
 * dashboard, so it only earns that weight when there is exactly one thing that
 * matters. Use the approvals list for anything countable.
 *
 * Two densities, both from Stitch: the compact form is `message` alone with a
 * chevron; passing `title`/`description`/`cta` gives the taller treatment with
 * a full-width call to action. The whole tile stays one anchor either way — the
 * CTA is a styled span, not a nested button, so it cannot trap focus or produce
 * invalid interactive nesting.
 */
export function AlertSpotlight({
  eyebrow = "System Alert",
  title,
  message,
  description,
  cta,
  href,
  tone = "warning",
  className,
}: {
  eyebrow?: string;
  /** Headline for the tall form. Omit for the compact one-liner. */
  title?: string;
  message: string;
  /** Supporting sentence under `message`. Only shown in the tall form. */
  description?: string;
  /** Label for the full-width action. Presence switches to the tall form. */
  cta?: string;
  href: string;
  tone?: "warning" | "error" | "info";
  className?: string;
}) {
  const icon =
    tone === "error" ? "error" : tone === "info" ? "info" : "warning";
  const iconColor =
    tone === "error"
      ? "text-error-container"
      : tone === "info"
        ? "text-primary-fixed-dim"
        : "text-amber-main";

  return (
    <Link
      href={href}
      className={cn(
        "group relative block overflow-hidden rounded-card bg-navy p-card-padding text-on-primary shadow-alert",
        className,
      )}
    >
      <div
        aria-hidden
        className="absolute -mr-10 -mt-10 right-0 top-0 size-32 rounded-full bg-primary/20 blur-2xl"
      />

      <div className="relative z-10 flex items-center justify-between gap-3">
        <span className="flex items-center gap-2 rounded bg-on-primary/10 px-2 py-1 text-[10px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wider">
          <Icon name={icon} className={cn("text-sm", iconColor)} />
          {eyebrow}
        </span>
        {cta ? null : (
          <Icon
            name="arrow_forward_ios"
            className="text-on-primary/50 transition-transform group-hover:translate-x-1"
          />
        )}
      </div>

      {title ? (
        <h3 className="relative z-10 mt-4 font-page-title text-page-title leading-tight">
          {title}
        </h3>
      ) : null}

      <p
        className={cn(
          "relative z-10 font-body-md text-body-md leading-tight",
          title ? "mt-2 opacity-80" : "mt-4 pr-4 font-medium",
        )}
      >
        {message}
      </p>

      {description ? (
        <p className="relative z-10 mt-2 font-body-md text-body-md leading-tight opacity-70">
          {description}
        </p>
      ) : null}

      {cta ? (
        <span className="relative z-10 mt-6 block w-full rounded-lg bg-on-primary py-2.5 text-center font-body-md text-body-md font-bold text-navy shadow-sm transition-colors group-hover:bg-surface-container-low">
          {cta}
        </span>
      ) : null}
    </Link>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/approval-list.tsx =====
import Link from "next/link";

import { initials } from "@/lib/format";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * "Awaiting Approval" queue — a compact list of pending items with the
 * requester, a type chip and a one-line summary.
 *
 * Avatars are initials, not photos: `employees.passport_photo_url` is
 * nullable, and a dashboard tile is the wrong place to fan out image requests.
 */

export type ApprovalTone = "leave" | "loan" | "advance" | "overtime" | "expense";

export type ApprovalItem = {
  id: string | number;
  requester: string;
  summary: string;
  /** Chip label, e.g. "Leave". */
  type: string;
  tone: ApprovalTone;
  href: string;
};

const TONE_CLASS: Record<ApprovalTone, string> = {
  leave: "text-amber-main bg-amber-main/10",
  loan: "text-blue bg-blue/10",
  advance: "text-blue bg-blue/10",
  overtime: "text-error bg-error/10",
  expense: "text-green-main bg-green-main/10",
};

export function ApprovalList({ items }: { items: readonly ApprovalItem[] }) {
  if (items.length === 0) {
    return (
      <p className="py-6 text-center text-[11px] text-outline">
        Nothing awaiting your approval.
      </p>
    );
  }

  return (
    <ul className="flex flex-col gap-1">
      {items.map((item) => (
        <li key={`${item.tone}-${item.id}`}>
          <Link
            href={item.href}
            className="group -mx-2 flex items-start gap-3 rounded-lg p-2 transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container"
          >
            <span
              aria-hidden
              className="flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-surface-container-highest text-[10px] font-bold text-on-surface-variant"
            >
              {initials(item.requester)}
            </span>

            <span className="min-w-0 flex-1">
              <span className="flex items-start justify-between gap-2">
                <span className="truncate text-[11px] font-semibold text-on-surface group-hover:text-primary-container">
                  {item.requester}
                </span>
                <span
                  className={cn(
                    "ml-2 shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap rounded px-1.5 py-0.5 text-[9px]",
                    TONE_CLASS[item.tone],
                  )}
                >
                  {item.type}
                </span>
              </span>
              <span className="mt-0.5 block truncate text-[10px] text-on-surface-variant">
                {item.summary}
              </span>
            </span>
          </Link>
        </li>
      ))}
    </ul>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/bar-chart.tsx =====
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Vertical bar chart, CSS-only.
 *
 * Deliberately not a charting library: these are small, fixed-height, and
 * render inside a Server Component with no interaction beyond a hover tooltip.
 * Pulling in recharts would ship ~100kB of client JS to draw seven divs.
 *
 * Heights are computed against `max` (defaults to the largest value) so the
 * tallest bar fills the plot area.
 */

export type BarDatum = {
  label: string;
  value: number;
  /** Highlights the bar in green with a glow — use for "current period". */
  highlight?: boolean;
  /** Overrides the tooltip text. Defaults to the raw value. */
  tooltip?: string;
};

export function BarChart({
  data,
  max,
  axisLabels,
  tone = "track",
  className,
}: {
  data: readonly BarDatum[];
  max?: number;
  /** Y-axis ticks, top to bottom. Rendered as-is, no scale derivation. */
  axisLabels?: readonly string[];
  /**
   * Colour of the non-highlighted bars. "track" is the muted grey used when one
   * bar carries the story; "primary" tints the whole series, for trends read as
   * a shape rather than a comparison.
   */
  tone?: "track" | "primary";
  className?: string;
}) {
  // Guard the divide: an all-zero series would otherwise produce NaN heights.
  const ceiling = max ?? Math.max(...data.map((d) => d.value), 1);

  return (
    <div className={cn("relative flex flex-1 items-end gap-2 pb-6 pl-8", className)}>
      {axisLabels?.length ? (
        <div
          aria-hidden
          className="absolute bottom-6 left-0 top-0 flex flex-col justify-between text-[9px] text-outline opacity-50"
        >
          {axisLabels.map((label) => (
            <span key={label}>{label}</span>
          ))}
        </div>
      ) : null}

      {/* Grid lines */}
      <div aria-hidden className="absolute inset-x-0 bottom-6 left-8 top-0">
        <div className="absolute inset-x-0 top-0 h-px bg-outline-variant/20" />
        <div className="absolute inset-x-0 top-1/3 h-px bg-outline-variant/20" />
        <div className="absolute inset-x-0 top-2/3 h-px bg-outline-variant/20" />
        <div className="absolute inset-x-0 bottom-0 h-px bg-outline-variant/40" />
      </div>

      {data.map((datum) => {
        const height = Math.max((datum.value / ceiling) * 100, 1);
        // The tooltip normally floats 32px above the bar, but a tall bar has no
        // headroom left inside the plot — it would escape the card and land on
        // the title. Past ~72% (the plot is ~117px in a 256px tile) flip it
        // inside the bar instead; the dark chip stays legible on the fill.
        const tallBar = height > 72;

        return (
          <div
            key={datum.label}
            className="group relative min-w-0 flex-1"
            style={{ height: `${height}%` }}
          >
            <div
              className={cn(
                "h-full w-full rounded-t-sm",
                datum.highlight
                  ? "z-10 bg-green-main shadow-[0_0_8px_rgb(59_154_47/0.4)]"
                  : tone === "primary"
                    ? "bg-primary-container opacity-80"
                    : "bg-chart-track",
              )}
            />

            <span
              role="tooltip"
              className={cn(
                "pointer-events-none absolute left-1/2 z-20 -translate-x-1/2 whitespace-nowrap rounded bg-on-surface px-2 py-1 text-[10px] text-on-primary opacity-0 transition-opacity group-hover:opacity-100",
                tallBar ? "top-1" : "-top-8",
              )}
            >
              {datum.tooltip ?? datum.value.toLocaleString()}
              {/* The pointer only makes sense when the chip floats above the
                  bar; sitting inside it, there is nothing to point at. */}
              {tallBar ? null : (
                <span
                  aria-hidden
                  className="absolute -bottom-1 left-1/2 size-2 -translate-x-1/2 rotate-45 bg-on-surface"
                />
              )}
            </span>

            <span
              className={cn(
                "absolute -bottom-5 left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2 text-[9px]",
                datum.highlight
                  ? "font-semibold text-on-surface"
                  : "text-outline",
              )}
            >
              {datum.label}
            </span>
          </div>
        );
      })}
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/bento-card.tsx =====
import type { ReactNode } from "react";

import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * White elevated surface — the base of every bento tile.
 *
 * `title` renders the standard header row with an optional right-hand slot
 * (filter button, "View All" link, period chip). It accepts any node so a tile
 * can lead with a glyph — `<span><Icon name="person" />Personal Details</span>`
 * — while the <h2> stays here, keeping the heading level consistent across
 * tiles. Omit it for tiles that own their whole interior, like the segment
 * strip or the alert spotlight.
 *
 * `flush` drops the body padding so a table or chart can bleed to the card
 * edges. The header keeps its inset, and the body then owns its own spacing —
 * see the device sync list, which re-applies `p-card-padding pt-0`.
 */
export function BentoCard({
  title,
  subtitle,
  action,
  children,
  className,
  bodyClassName,
  flush = false,
}: {
  title?: ReactNode;
  subtitle?: string;
  action?: ReactNode;
  children: ReactNode;
  className?: string;
  bodyClassName?: string;
  flush?: boolean;
}) {
  return (
    <section
      className={cn(
        // min-w-0 lets a card inside a grid track actually shrink; without it a
        // wide table sets the track's minimum and the whole row overflows.
        "bento-card min-w-0",
        flush || "p-4 sm:p-card-padding",
        className,
      )}
    >
      {title ? (
        <header
          className={cn(
            // Wraps so a long title plus an action chip stack on narrow cards
            // instead of crushing each other.
            "mb-4 flex flex-wrap items-start justify-between gap-x-3 gap-y-2",
            flush && "px-4 pt-4 sm:px-card-padding sm:pt-card-padding",
          )}
        >
          <div className="min-w-0 flex-1">
            <h2 className="font-body-md text-body-md font-semibold text-on-surface">
              {title}
            </h2>
            {subtitle ? (
              <p className="mt-1 text-[10px] text-outline">{subtitle}</p>
            ) : null}
          </div>
          {action ? <div className="shrink-0">{action}</div> : null}
        </header>
      ) : null}

      {/* `min-h-0` is the vertical counterpart to `min-w-0`: a flex item's
          automatic minimum size is its content, so without it a `flex-1` chart
          body cannot shrink inside a height-capped card — it keeps its content
          height and the overflow spills onto the card below. */}
      <div className={cn("min-h-0 min-w-0", bodyClassName)}>{children}</div>
    </section>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/brand-logo.tsx =====
import Image from "next/image";

import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * The supplied brand asset: one 684x673 JPEG holding the whole lockup — the
 * three-figure emblem stacked above the "Dhwarsh / Employee Management"
 * wordmark. There is no separate mark-only or transparent file, so both
 * components below are views onto this single source.
 */
export const BRAND_LOGO_SRC = "/brand/dhwarsh-logo.jpg";
const BRAND_LOGO_WIDTH = 684;
const BRAND_LOGO_HEIGHT = 673;

/** Accessible name. The asset carries the product name as artwork, so any
 *  standalone use has to restate it in text for screen readers. */
export const BRAND_LOGO_ALT = "Dhwarsh Employee Management";

/**
 * The emblem on its own, cropped out of the lockup in CSS.
 *
 * Square slots — the nav rail, the auth panels, anything under ~64px — cannot
 * use the full lockup: at that size the wordmark is an illegible smudge. The
 * crop is done with layout rather than a second hand-trimmed binary so there
 * is one asset to replace when branding changes.
 *
 * How it works: the image is over-sized inside an `overflow-hidden` box and
 * offset so the emblem's bounding box in the source (x 7%..97.5%,
 * y 2.5%..65.5%) lands flush against the box's width and centred on its
 * height. Those offsets are percentages of the wrapper, so the crop survives
 * any size change — but only while the wrapper is square, since `top` resolves
 * against height and the rest against width. Callers must pass a square size.
 *
 * The JPEG's own near-white background becomes the visible backdrop (the image
 * covers the box entirely), which is why every caller puts it on a light tile
 * rather than letting it sit raw on a coloured panel.
 */
export function BrandMark({
  className,
  alt = "",
  eager = false,
  sizes = "64px",
}: {
  /** Must resolve to a square box, e.g. `size-11`. Set the radius here too. */
  className?: string;
  /** Leave empty when adjacent text already names the brand. */
  alt?: string;
  /** Skip lazy-loading for above-the-fold use (auth panels, the rail). */
  eager?: boolean;
  sizes?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <span
      className={cn(
        "relative block shrink-0 overflow-hidden bg-white",
        className,
      )}
    >
      <Image
        src={BRAND_LOGO_SRC}
        alt={alt}
        width={BRAND_LOGO_WIDTH}
        height={BRAND_LOGO_HEIGHT}
        sizes={sizes}
        loading={eager ? "eager" : undefined}
        /* w-/left-/top- override the width and height attributes: the box, not
           the intrinsic size, drives the rendered geometry. `max-w-none` is
           load-bearing — the base reset's `max-width: 100%` would otherwise
           cancel the 110.5% overscale and undo the crop. */
        className="absolute left-[-7.7%] top-[13%] h-auto w-[110.5%] max-w-none"
      />
    </span>
  );
}

/**
 * The complete lockup, emblem and wordmark together.
 *
 * For places with room to show the brand properly — the login form panel, the
 * standalone auth cards. Keep it at 120px wide or more or the wordmark stops
 * being readable and you want {@link BrandMark} instead.
 *
 * Renders edge-to-edge by design: the artwork carries its own margins, and the
 * asset's near-white ground would otherwise show as a visible square against a
 * pure-white tile.
 */
export function BrandLockup({
  className,
  eager = false,
  sizes = "240px",
}: {
  className?: string;
  eager?: boolean;
  sizes?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <Image
      src={BRAND_LOGO_SRC}
      alt={BRAND_LOGO_ALT}
      width={BRAND_LOGO_WIDTH}
      height={BRAND_LOGO_HEIGHT}
      sizes={sizes}
      loading={eager ? "eager" : undefined}
      className={cn("h-auto w-full object-contain", className)}
    />
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/charts.tsx =====
import { formatPercent } from "@/lib/format";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Horizontal stacked bar with a legend underneath.
 *
 * Segments are proportional to their own values — the caller passes counts,
 * not percentages, so the widths cannot drift out of sync with the legend
 * numbers shown beside them.
 */

export type StackSegment = {
  label: string;
  value: number;
  /** Any CSS colour. Use a token: `var(--color-blue)`. */
  color: string;
};

export function StackedBar({
  segments,
  className,
}: {
  segments: readonly StackSegment[];
  className?: string;
}) {
  const total = segments.reduce((sum, s) => sum + s.value, 0);

  return (
    <div className={cn("w-full", className)}>
      <div className="flex h-3 w-full overflow-hidden rounded-full shadow-inner">
        {segments.map((segment) => (
          <div
            key={segment.label}
            className="h-full"
            style={{
              width: total > 0 ? `${(segment.value / total) * 100}%` : "0%",
              backgroundColor: segment.color,
            }}
          />
        ))}
      </div>

      {/* Wraps instead of compressing: with five segments on a 360px screen a
          single justified row shrinks each label past legibility. */}
      <ul className="mt-4 flex flex-wrap items-start justify-between gap-x-3 gap-y-2 px-1 sm:px-2">
        {segments.map((segment) => (
          <li key={segment.label} className="flex min-w-0 flex-col items-center">
            <div className="flex items-center gap-1">
              <span
                aria-hidden
                className="size-2 shrink-0 rounded-full"
                style={{ backgroundColor: segment.color }}
              />
              <span className="text-[10px] font-semibold tabular-nums text-on-surface">
                {segment.value.toLocaleString()}
              </span>
            </div>
            <span className="mt-0.5 text-[9px] text-outline">{segment.label}</span>
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * Donut split, drawn with a conic-gradient over a punched-out centre.
 *
 * Same rationale as BarChart — no client JS for what is one gradient and a
 * legend. Percentages are derived from the segment values.
 *
 * `centerLabel` fills the punched-out middle with the total; `legend: "value"`
 * shows raw counts instead of percentages. Stitch uses both treatments — the
 * admin workforce split shows percentages, the HR employment-type card shows
 * headcounts against a total in the middle.
 */
export function DonutChart({
  segments,
  size = 96,
  legend = "percent",
  centerLabel,
  className,
}: {
  segments: readonly StackSegment[];
  /**
   * Intended diameter in px. Treated as a ceiling, not a fixed size — the ring
   * shrinks to fit a card narrower than this and stays circular as it does.
   */
  size?: number;
  legend?: "percent" | "value";
  /** Caption under the centre total, e.g. "Total". Omit to leave it empty. */
  centerLabel?: string;
  className?: string;
}) {
  const total = segments.reduce((sum, s) => sum + s.value, 0) || 1;

  // conic-gradient wants cumulative stops: `colour <from>% <to>%`. Each offset
  // is derived from a prefix sum rather than a mutable cursor, so the callback
  // stays pure; with three to five segments the repeated sum costs nothing.
  const stops = segments
    .map((segment, index) => {
      const before = segments
        .slice(0, index)
        .reduce((sum, s) => sum + s.value, 0);
      const from = (before / total) * 100;
      const to = from + (segment.value / total) * 100;
      return `${segment.color} ${from}% ${to}%`;
    })
    .join(", ");

  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        centerLabel
          ? "flex flex-col items-center gap-4"
          : // Wraps so the legend drops below the ring rather than squashing:
            // in a 4-col side card at 1024px with the rail expanded there is
            // only ~170px to share, and a truncated "Housing Levy" is useless.
            "flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4",
        className,
      )}
    >
      {/* `size` is the intended diameter, not a hard one — `max-width` lets it
          shrink inside a narrow card and `aspect-ratio` keeps it circular as it
          does. Setting an explicit height here would override the ratio. */}
      <div
        className="relative flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full"
        style={{
          width: size,
          maxWidth: "100%",
          aspectRatio: "1",
          background: `conic-gradient(${stops})`,
        }}
      >
        <div className="absolute flex h-[66%] w-[66%] flex-col items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-surface-container-lowest shadow-inner">
          {centerLabel ? (
            <>
              <span className="font-card-stat text-card-stat leading-none tabular-nums text-on-surface">
                {total.toLocaleString()}
              </span>
              <span className="mt-1 text-[10px] text-on-surface-variant">
                {centerLabel}
              </span>
            </>
          ) : null}
        </div>
      </div>

      <ul
        className={cn(
          "flex flex-col justify-center gap-2",
          centerLabel ? "w-full gap-3" : "min-w-28 flex-1",
        )}
      >
        {segments.map((segment) => (
          <li key={segment.label} className="flex items-center justify-between gap-2">
            <div className="flex min-w-0 items-center gap-1.5">
              <span
                aria-hidden
                className="size-2 shrink-0 rounded-full"
                style={{ backgroundColor: segment.color }}
              />
              <span className="min-w-0 truncate text-[10px] font-medium text-on-surface">
                {segment.label}
              </span>
            </div>
            {legend === "value" ? (
              <span className="shrink-0 text-[10px] font-bold tabular-nums text-on-surface">
                {segment.value.toLocaleString()}
              </span>
            ) : (
              <span
                className="shrink-0 text-[10px] font-bold tabular-nums"
                style={{ color: segment.color }}
              >
                {Math.round((segment.value / total) * 100)}%
              </span>
            )}
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * Labelled progress rows — "Leave Requests 75%" over a filled track.
 *
 * Not a chart: each row is an independent ratio, so they do not sum to a whole
 * the way StackedBar's segments do. Values are whole percentages.
 */
export function ProgressRows({
  rows,
  className,
}: {
  rows: readonly { label: string; value: number; color: string }[];
  className?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <div className={cn("flex flex-col justify-center gap-4", className)}>
      {rows.map((row) => (
        <div key={row.label}>
          <div className="mb-1 flex items-center justify-between font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant">
            <span>{row.label}</span>
            <span className="font-semibold tabular-nums text-on-surface">
              {formatPercent(row.value)}
            </span>
          </div>
          <div className="h-2 w-full overflow-hidden rounded-full bg-surface-container-high">
            <div
              className="h-full rounded-full transition-[width]"
              style={{
                width: `${Math.min(Math.max(row.value, 0), 100)}%`,
                backgroundColor: row.color,
              }}
            />
          </div>
        </div>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/data-unavailable.tsx =====
import { PageHeader } from "@/components/shared/page-header";
import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";

/**
 * Shown when a portal page cannot reach the API at all.
 *
 * This exists because of a specific failure mode: the screen that renders
 * zeros is indistinguishable from the screen that renders zeros because
 * nothing happened. An administrator looking at an attendance board showing
 * "0 present" cannot tell whether the backend is down or nobody came to work,
 * and those two readings call for opposite actions.
 *
 * So a failed read renders this instead of an empty table — the page refuses
 * to imply a fact it could not establish. The header is kept so the route is
 * still identifiable, but its metric slots are left empty rather than zeroed.
 *
 * Long-form `reason` is for the underlying error text. The last line is the
 * first-run case: `API_BASE_URL` in `.env.local` pointing at a backend that
 * is not running is the most common cause by a wide margin.
 */
export function DataUnavailable({
  title,
  description,
  subject,
  reason,
}: {
  /** Page title, matching the header the page renders on success. */
  title: string;
  /** Page description, matching the header the page renders on success. */
  description?: string;
  /**
   * What could not be loaded, in the reader's terms — "Branches", "the
   * payroll run list". Used inside the sentence, so keep it a noun phrase.
   */
  subject: string;
  /** The underlying error message. */
  reason?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <>
      <PageHeader title={title} description={description} />

      <div className="rounded-card border border-dashed border-error/40 bg-error/5 p-8 text-center">
        <Icon name="cloud_off" className="text-[40px] text-error" />
        <p className="mt-3 font-body-md text-body-md font-semibold text-on-surface">
          {subject} could not be loaded
        </p>
        {reason ? (
          <p className="mx-auto mt-1 max-w-md font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant">
            {reason}
          </p>
        ) : null}
        <p className="mx-auto mt-3 max-w-md text-[11px] text-outline">
          Check that the backend is running and that <code>API_BASE_URL</code> in{" "}
          <code>.env.local</code> matches the port it listens on.
        </p>
      </div>
    </>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/empty-state.tsx =====
import type { LucideIcon } from "lucide-react";
import type { ReactNode } from "react";

import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Shown when a list or table has no rows.
 *
 * Token note: this previously used shadcn's default palette (`border-border`,
 * `bg-muted`, `text-muted-foreground`). None of those are defined in this
 * project's `@theme` block, so they compiled to nothing and every empty state
 * rendered with an invisible border and unstyled text. It now uses the same
 * Material tokens as the rest of the app.
 *
 * Takes either a Lucide component (`icon`) or a Material Symbols ligature
 * (`symbol`) — the app uses the icon font nearly everywhere, but the original
 * signature took a Lucide icon, so both keep working.
 */
export function EmptyState({
  title,
  description,
  icon: LucideGlyph,
  symbol,
  action,
  className,
}: {
  title: string;
  description?: string;
  icon?: LucideIcon;
  /** Material Symbols ligature, e.g. "inbox". Takes precedence over `icon`. */
  symbol?: string;
  action?: ReactNode;
  className?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "flex flex-col items-center justify-center rounded-card border border-dashed border-outline-variant px-4 py-10 text-center sm:px-6 sm:py-16",
        className,
      )}
    >
      {symbol || LucideGlyph ? (
        <div className="mb-4 flex size-11 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-surface-container">
          {symbol ? (
            <Icon name={symbol} className="text-[22px] text-on-surface-variant" />
          ) : LucideGlyph ? (
            <LucideGlyph className="size-5 text-on-surface-variant" aria-hidden />
          ) : null}
        </div>
      ) : null}

      <p className="font-body-md text-sm font-semibold text-on-surface">{title}</p>
      {description ? (
        <p className="mt-1 max-w-sm font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant">
          {description}
        </p>
      ) : null}
      {action ? <div className="mt-5">{action}</div> : null}
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/filter-tabs.tsx =====
import Link from "next/link";

import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Segmented filter row above a list.
 *
 * Links, not buttons: the active filter belongs in the URL so a filtered list
 * is shareable, survives a refresh, and the back button steps through filter
 * changes. The page reads the same value from `searchParams`.
 */

export type FilterTab = {
  label: string;
  /** Value written to the query param. `undefined` clears it (the "All" tab). */
  value?: string;
  count?: number;
};

export function FilterTabs({
  tabs,
  active,
  param = "filter",
  basePath,
  preserve,
  className,
}: {
  tabs: readonly FilterTab[];
  /** Currently selected value, from searchParams. */
  active?: string;
  param?: string;
  /** Route the tabs link to, e.g. "/admin/branches". */
  basePath: string;
  /**
   * Other query params to carry across a tab switch, e.g. `{ branchId: "3", date }`.
   * Without this a tab is a link to a fresh query string, so switching tabs on a
   * filtered list silently resets every other filter. Keys that collide with
   * `param` are ignored — the tab owns its own.
   */
  preserve?: Record<string, string | undefined>;
  className?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <div className={cn("flex flex-wrap gap-2", className)}>
      {tabs.map((tab) => {
        const selected = tab.value === active;

        const params = new URLSearchParams();
        if (tab.value) params.set(param, tab.value);
        for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(preserve ?? {})) {
          if (value && key !== param) params.set(key, value);
        }

        const query = params.toString();
        const href = query ? `${basePath}?${query}` : basePath;

        return (
          <Link
            key={tab.label}
            href={href}
            aria-current={selected ? "true" : undefined}
            className={cn(
              "rounded-md px-4 py-1.5 font-body-md text-body-md transition-colors",
              selected
                ? "border border-surface-container-high bg-surface-container-low font-semibold text-primary"
                : "bg-transparent text-on-surface-variant hover:bg-surface-container-low",
            )}
          >
            {tab.label}
            {tab.count !== undefined ? (
              <span className="ml-1.5 tabular-nums opacity-60">{tab.count}</span>
            ) : null}
          </Link>
        );
      })}
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/metric-card.tsx =====
import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * White KPI tile with a tinted icon chip.
 *
 * The counterpart to `StatCard`: that one is the filled primary tile that opens
 * a dashboard, this one is the lighter tile Stitch uses for counts that need to
 * sit together without three blocks of solid blue competing for attention.
 *
 * `accent` adds the coloured left edge Stitch puts on tiles that carry a
 * deadline ("Probation Ending", "Docs Expiring"). Leave it off for neutral
 * counts — the edge is a severity signal, not decoration.
 */

export type MetricTone = "primary" | "error" | "tertiary" | "amber";

const TONE: Record<MetricTone, { chip: string; edge: string }> = {
  primary: {
    chip: "bg-blue-soft text-primary-container",
    edge: "border-l-4 border-primary-container",
  },
  error: {
    chip: "bg-error-container/30 text-error",
    edge: "border-l-4 border-error",
  },
  tertiary: {
    chip: "bg-tertiary-container/20 text-tertiary",
    edge: "border-l-4 border-tertiary",
  },
  amber: {
    chip: "bg-amber-main/15 text-amber-main",
    edge: "border-l-4 border-amber-main",
  },
};

export function MetricCard({
  label,
  value,
  icon,
  tone = "primary",
  accent = false,
  hint,
  trend,
  className,
}: {
  label: string;
  /** Pre-formatted. Use formatNumber/formatMoney at the call site. */
  value: string;
  /** Material Symbols ligature for the chip. */
  icon: string;
  tone?: MetricTone;
  /** Draws the coloured left edge. Reserve it for time-sensitive counts. */
  accent?: boolean;
  hint?: string;
  /** Signed movement, e.g. "+2.4% this month". Direction comes from the sign. */
  trend?: string;
  className?: string;
}) {
  const styles = TONE[tone];
  const rising = trend?.trimStart().startsWith("+");

  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        // .bento-card already carries the white fill, radius, ambient shadow
        // and hover lift — do not also add .card-shadow, they duplicate.
        "bento-card flex min-w-0 flex-col justify-between p-4 sm:p-card-padding",
        accent && styles.edge,
        className,
      )}
    >
      <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-2">
        <p className="min-w-0 font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant">
          {label}
        </p>
        <span
          className={cn(
            "flex size-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full",
            styles.chip,
          )}
        >
          <Icon name={icon} className="text-sm" />
        </span>
      </div>

      {/* `break-words` rather than `truncate`: a clipped currency figure is
          worse than a wrapped one — "KES 1,2…" is actively misleading. */}
      <p className="mt-4 break-words font-card-stat text-card-stat tabular-nums text-on-surface">
        {value}
      </p>

      {trend ? (
        <p
          className={cn(
            "mt-2 flex items-center gap-1 text-[10px]",
            rising ? "text-green-main" : "text-error",
          )}
        >
          <Icon
            name={rising ? "trending_up" : "trending_down"}
            className="text-[14px]"
          />
          {trend}
        </p>
      ) : null}

      {hint ? (
        <p className="mt-2 text-[10px] text-on-surface-variant">{hint}</p>
      ) : null}
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/page-header.tsx =====
import type { ReactNode } from "react";

/**
 * Standard page heading for list and detail screens.
 *
 * Dashboards do not use this — their title lives in the top bar greeting.
 *
 * `title` accepts any node so a detail screen can prefix a back-link, but the
 * <h1> lives here — pass the content, never your own heading element.
 *
 * `pill` is for a live count or status beside the title ("9 Active"), not for
 * arbitrary chrome; it sits inside the <h1> so screen readers announce it with
 * the heading.
 */
export function PageHeader({
  title,
  description,
  pill,
  actions,
}: {
  title: ReactNode;
  description?: string;
  pill?: ReactNode;
  actions?: ReactNode;
}) {
  return (
    <div className="mb-6 flex flex-col gap-3 sm:mb-8 sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap sm:items-end sm:justify-between sm:gap-4">
      <div className="min-w-0">
        <h1 className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 font-display-greeting text-lg text-primary sm:gap-3 sm:text-display-greeting">
          {title}
          {pill}
        </h1>
        {description ? (
          <p className="mt-1.5 font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface-variant sm:mt-2">
            {description}
          </p>
        ) : null}
      </div>

      {/* Wraps rather than shrinking: a row of action buttons squeezed onto a
          360px screen becomes a row of unreadable slivers. */}
      {actions ? (
        <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 sm:shrink-0">
          {actions}
        </div>
      ) : null}
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * Count/status chip for PageHeader's `pill` slot.
 *
 * The leading dot is decorative — tone alone must never be the only carrier of
 * meaning, so `label` always spells it out ("9 Active", not a bare "9").
 */
export function CountPill({
  label,
  tone = "success",
}: {
  label: string;
  tone?: "success" | "warning" | "error" | "neutral";
}) {
  const styles = {
    success:
      "border-tertiary-container/20 bg-tertiary-container/10 text-tertiary [--dot:var(--color-tertiary)]",
    warning:
      "border-amber-main/20 bg-amber-main/10 text-amber-main [--dot:var(--color-amber-main)]",
    error: "border-error/20 bg-error/10 text-error [--dot:var(--color-error)]",
    neutral:
      "border-outline-variant/40 bg-surface-container text-on-surface-variant [--dot:var(--color-outline)]",
  }[tone];

  return (
    <span
      className={`flex items-center gap-1 rounded-full border px-3 py-1 text-[11px] font-bold ${styles}`}
    >
      <span
        aria-hidden
        className="block size-1.5 rounded-full bg-[var(--dot)]"
      />
      {label}
    </span>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/pagination.tsx =====
import Link from "next/link";

import { formatNumber } from "@/lib/format";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Previous / "Page N of M" / Next row for a paginated table.
 *
 * The controls are links rather than buttons on purpose: the page number
 * belongs in the URL alongside the other filters, so a page is shareable,
 * bookmarkable and survives a refresh. That means pagination has to be
 * rendered from a Server Component, which is why this takes `hrefFor` instead
 * of an `onPageChange` callback.
 *
 * Renders nothing when there is a single page — a lone "Page 1 of 1" is noise.
 *
 * `pageNumber` is the zero-based index the API returns; the label is
 * one-based because that is what a reader expects.
 */
export function Pagination<T>({
  data,
  hrefFor,
  className,
}: {
  data: {
    pageNumber: number;
    totalPages: number;
    last: boolean;
  };
  /** Builds the URL for a zero-based page index. */
  hrefFor: (page: number) => string;
  className?: string;
}) {
  if (data.totalPages <= 1) return null;

  const hasPrevious = data.pageNumber > 0;
  const hasNext = !data.last;

  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "mt-4 flex items-center justify-between border-t border-outline-variant/40 pt-4 text-[11px] text-on-surface-variant",
        className,
      )}
    >
      <span>
        Page {formatNumber(data.pageNumber + 1)} of {formatNumber(data.totalPages)}
      </span>

      <div className="flex items-center gap-1">
        {hasPrevious ? (
          <Link
            href={hrefFor(data.pageNumber - 1)}
            rel="prev"
            className="rounded-md px-2.5 py-1 transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container-low"
          >
            Previous
          </Link>
        ) : null}

        {hasNext ? (
          <Link
            href={hrefFor(data.pageNumber + 1)}
            rel="next"
            className="rounded-md px-2.5 py-1 transition-colors hover:bg-surface-container-low"
          >
            Next
          </Link>
        ) : null}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/password-field.tsx =====
"use client";

import { useState } from "react";

import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Password field with a show/hide toggle.
 *
 * Stitch drives this with an inline DOMContentLoaded script; as a React
 * component it becomes the local state below. Shared by the staff and agent
 * login screens, which render the control identically apart from the leading
 * icon.
 */
export function PasswordField({
  id = "password",
  name = "password",
  placeholder = "••••••••",
  required,
  autoComplete = "current-password",
  invalid,
  leadingIcon,
  className,
}: {
  id?: string;
  name?: string;
  placeholder?: string;
  required?: boolean;
  autoComplete?: string;
  invalid?: boolean;
  /** Material Symbols ligature rendered inside the field, e.g. "lock". */
  leadingIcon?: string;
  className?: string;
}) {
  const [visible, setVisible] = useState(false);

  return (
    <div className="relative flex items-center">
      {leadingIcon ? (
        <Icon
          name={leadingIcon}
          className="absolute left-3 text-[18px] text-outline"
        />
      ) : null}

      <input
        id={id}
        name={name}
        type={visible ? "text" : "password"}
        placeholder={placeholder}
        required={required}
        autoComplete={autoComplete}
        aria-invalid={invalid || undefined}
        className={cn(
          "w-full rounded-lg border bg-surface-container-lowest font-body-md text-body-md text-on-surface transition-all",
          "placeholder:text-outline-variant focus:outline-none focus:ring-1",
          leadingIcon ? "pl-10" : "pl-3",
          "pr-10",
          invalid
            ? "border-error focus:border-error focus:ring-error/30"
            : "border-outline-variant focus:border-primary focus:ring-primary/30",
          className,
        )}
      />

      <button
        type="button"
        onClick={() => setVisible((v) => !v)}
        aria-label={visible ? "Hide password" : "Show password"}
        className="absolute right-3 text-outline transition-colors hover:text-primary focus:outline-none"
      >
        <Icon
          name={visible ? "visibility" : "visibility_off"}
          className="text-[18px]"
        />
      </button>
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/pin-input.tsx =====
"use client";

import { useRef, useState, type ClipboardEvent, type KeyboardEvent } from "react";

import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

const PIN_LENGTH = 4;

/**
 * Four-box PIN entry with auto-advance and backspace-retreat.
 *
 * Replaces the inline querySelectorAll script in the Stitch export. Beyond
 * parity it adds paste support — pasting "1234" fills all four boxes instead
 * of dropping three characters, which the original silently did.
 *
 * The joined value is submitted via a single hidden input so the Server Action
 * receives one `pin` field rather than four.
 */
export function PinInput({
  name = "pin",
  invalid,
  disabled,
}: {
  name?: string;
  invalid?: boolean;
  disabled?: boolean;
}) {
  const [digits, setDigits] = useState<string[]>(() =>
    Array<string>(PIN_LENGTH).fill(""),
  );
  const refs = useRef<Array<HTMLInputElement | null>>([]);

  function focusBox(index: number) {
    refs.current[index]?.focus();
    refs.current[index]?.select();
  }

  function setDigit(index: number, value: string) {
    setDigits((prev) => {
      const next = [...prev];
      next[index] = value;
      return next;
    });
  }

  function handleChange(index: number, raw: string) {
    // Keep only the last digit typed, so overwriting a filled box works.
    const digit = raw.replace(/\D/g, "").slice(-1);

    if (!digit) {
      setDigit(index, "");
      return;
    }

    setDigit(index, digit);

    if (index < PIN_LENGTH - 1) {
      focusBox(index + 1);
    }
  }

  function handleKeyDown(index: number, event: KeyboardEvent<HTMLInputElement>) {
    if (event.key === "Backspace" && !digits[index] && index > 0) {
      event.preventDefault();
      setDigit(index - 1, "");
      focusBox(index - 1);
      return;
    }

    if (event.key === "ArrowLeft" && index > 0) {
      event.preventDefault();
      focusBox(index - 1);
      return;
    }

    if (event.key === "ArrowRight" && index < PIN_LENGTH - 1) {
      event.preventDefault();
      focusBox(index + 1);
    }
  }

  function handlePaste(event: ClipboardEvent<HTMLInputElement>) {
    const pasted = event.clipboardData
      .getData("text")
      .replace(/\D/g, "")
      .slice(0, PIN_LENGTH);

    if (!pasted) return;

    event.preventDefault();

    const next = Array<string>(PIN_LENGTH).fill("");
    for (let i = 0; i < pasted.length; i += 1) {
      next[i] = pasted[i]!;
    }
    setDigits(next);
    focusBox(Math.min(pasted.length, PIN_LENGTH - 1));
  }

  return (
    <>
      <input type="hidden" name={name} value={digits.join("")} />

      <div className="flex justify-between gap-3">
        {digits.map((digit, index) => (
          <input
            // Fixed-length control; index is a stable identity here.
            key={index}
            ref={(el) => {
              refs.current[index] = el;
            }}
            type="password"
            inputMode="numeric"
            autoComplete="one-time-code"
            maxLength={1}
            value={digit}
            disabled={disabled}
            aria-label={`PIN digit ${index + 1}`}
            aria-invalid={invalid || undefined}
            onChange={(e) => handleChange(index, e.target.value)}
            onKeyDown={(e) => handleKeyDown(index, e)}
            onPaste={handlePaste}
            onFocus={(e) => e.target.select()}
            className={cn(
              "h-[56px] w-[52px] rounded-pin border bg-surface-container-lowest text-center",
              "font-display-greeting text-display-greeting text-navy shadow-sm",
              "transition-all focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:border-transparent",
              "disabled:cursor-not-allowed disabled:opacity-60",
              invalid
                ? "border-error focus:ring-error"
                : "border-line focus:ring-blue",
            )}
          />
        ))}
      </div>
    </>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/segment-strip.tsx =====
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Divided strip of counts — the daily attendance summary tile.
 *
 * Equal-width cells with hairline dividers, no card header. Values are
 * pre-formatted by the caller.
 */

export type Segment = {
  label: string;
  value: string;
  /** Tailwind text colour class. Defaults to the neutral on-surface. */
  tone?: "default" | "warning" | "error" | "success";
};

const TONE_CLASS: Record<NonNullable<Segment["tone"]>, string> = {
  default: "text-on-surface",
  warning: "text-amber-main",
  error: "text-error",
  success: "text-green-main",
};

export function SegmentStrip({
  segments,
  className,
}: {
  segments: readonly Segment[];
  className?: string;
}) {
  return (
    <div
      className={cn(
        "card-shadow flex divide-x divide-outline-variant/20 overflow-hidden rounded-card bg-surface-container-lowest",
        className,
      )}
    >
      {segments.map((segment) => (
        <div
          key={segment.label}
          className="flex flex-1 flex-col items-center justify-center py-4"
        >
          <span className="mb-1 max-w-full truncate px-1 text-[10px] text-outline">{segment.label}</span>
          <span
            className={cn(
              "max-w-full truncate px-1 font-body-md text-body-md font-bold tabular-nums",
              TONE_CLASS[segment.tone ?? "default"],
            )}
          >
            {segment.value}
          </span>
        </div>
      ))}
    </div>
  );
}

===== src/components/shared/status-badge.tsx =====
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import { humanizeEnum } from "@/lib/format";

/**
 * Status pill covering every status enum in the schema — employment, leave,
 * loan, payroll run, attendance, advance, device.
 *
 * Grouped by meaning rather than by module, so a PENDING loan and a PENDING
 * leave request read identically to the user.
 *
 * Token note: the neutral tone previously used shadcn's `bg-muted` /
 * `text-muted-foreground`, which are not defined in this project's `@theme`
 * block — neutral pills rendered as unstyled text on no background. The tones
 * below use the app's own Material palette so every status is legible.
 */

type Tone = "neutral" | "success" | "warning" | "danger" | "info";

const TONE_CLASS: Record<Tone, string> = {
  neutral: "bg-surface-container text-on-surface-variant",
  success: "bg-tertiary-container/15 text-tertiary",
  warning: "bg-amber-main/15 text-amber-main",
  danger: "bg-error-container/40 text-error",
  info: "bg-blue-soft text-primary-container",
};

const STATUS_TONE: Record<string, Tone> = {
  // Positive terminal states
  ACTIVE: "success",
  APPROVED: "success",
  PRESENT: "success",
  COMPLETED: "success",
  SETTLED: "success",
  RECOVERED: "success",
  ONLINE: "success",

  // Awaiting action
  PENDING: "warning",
  PENDING_HR: "warning",
  PROCESSING: "warning",
  PROBATION: "warning",
  LATE: "warning",
  HALF_DAY: "warning",
  OUTSTANDING: "warning",
  DRAFT: "warning",
  /** Clocked in, not yet out — the shift is still open. */
  IN_PROGRESS: "warning",

  // Attendance events. A scan is not a state the employee is "in" — it is the
  // moment they arrived or left — but it is what the live feed shows, so it
  // needs a tone. In is the positive direction, out is simply recorded.
  CLOCK_IN: "success",
  CLOCK_OUT: "info",

  // Negative terminal states
  REJECTED: "danger",
  TERMINATED: "danger",
  SUSPENDED: "danger",
  ABSENT: "danger",
  FAILED: "danger",
  DISABLED: "danger",

  // Informational
  ON_LEAVE: "info",
  HOLIDAY: "info",
  CANCELLED: "neutral",
  INACTIVE: "neutral",
  OFFLINE: "neutral",
};

export function StatusBadge({
  status,
  className,
}: {
  status: string | null | undefined;
  className?: string;
}) {
  if (!status) {
    return <span className="text-sm text-on-surface-variant">—</span>;
  }

  const tone = STATUS_TONE[status] ?? "neutral";

  return (
    <span
      className={cn(
        "inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2.5 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium",
        TONE_CLASS[tone],
        className,
      )}
    >
      {humanizeEnum(status)}
    </span>
  );
}

===== src/components/ui/button-variants.ts =====
import { cva, type VariantProps } from "class-variance-authority";

/**
 * Button styling, shared by the Server-Component markup that only needs classes
 * and the Client-Component <Button> that needs behaviour.
 *
 * Kept in its own module with no "use client" so importing the classes from a
 * Server Component does not drag a client bundle along with them.
 *
 * Sizes are touch-first: `h-10` is 40px, and the `min-w` floors stop an
 * icon-only button collapsing below the ~44px pointer target on a phone.
 */
export const buttonVariants = cva(
  [
    "inline-flex items-center justify-center gap-2 whitespace-nowrap rounded-lg",
    "font-body-md text-body-md font-semibold transition-all",
    "focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-primary/40 focus-visible:ring-offset-1",
    "disabled:pointer-events-none disabled:opacity-60",
  ].join(" "),
  {
    variants: {
      variant: {
        primary: "bg-primary text-on-primary shadow-ambient-1 hover:bg-primary-container",
        secondary:
          "bg-surface-container text-on-surface hover:bg-surface-container-high",
        outline:
          "border border-outline-variant bg-surface-container-lowest text-on-surface hover:bg-surface-container-low",
        ghost: "text-on-surface-variant hover:bg-surface-container-low hover:text-on-surface",
        danger: "bg-error text-on-error hover:bg-error/90",
      },
      size: {
        sm: "h-8 min-w-8 px-3 text-[11px]",
        md: "h-10 min-w-10 px-4",
        lg: "h-11 min-w-11 px-6",
        icon: "size-10 shrink-0 rounded-full p-0",
      },
      block: {
        true: "w-full",
        false: "",
      },
    },
    defaultVariants: {
      variant: "primary",
      size: "md",
      block: false,
    },
  },
);

export type ButtonVariantProps = VariantProps<typeof buttonVariants>;

===== src/components/ui/button.tsx =====
"use client";

import { forwardRef, type ButtonHTMLAttributes, type ReactNode } from "react";
import { useFormStatus } from "react-dom";

import { Icon } from "@/components/ui/icon";
import { buttonVariants, type ButtonVariantProps } from "@/components/ui/button-variants";
import { toast } from "@/lib/toast";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

type BaseProps = ButtonHTMLAttributes<HTMLButtonElement> &
  ButtonVariantProps & {
    /** Replaces the label with a spinner and blocks re-entry. */
    loading?: boolean;
    /** Announced to screen readers while `loading`. */
    loadingText?: string;
    icon?: string;
    children?: ReactNode;
  };

/**
 * Interactive button.
 *
 * Exists mainly so the loading state is not re-invented per form: a disabled
 * button with no spinner reads as "broken", and a spinner with no `disabled`
 * lets an impatient user submit payroll twice.
 */
export const Button = forwardRef<HTMLButtonElement, BaseProps>(function Button(
  { className, variant, size, block, loading, loadingText, icon, children, disabled, ...props },
  ref,
) {
  return (
    <button
      ref={ref}
      // Live by construction — see StubActionBridge for why this matters.
      data-live=""
      disabled={disabled || loading}
      aria-busy={loading || undefined}
      className={cn(buttonVariants({ variant, size, block }), className)}
      {...props}
    >
      {loading ? (
        <>
          <Icon name="progress_activity" className="animate-spin text-[18px]" />
          <span>{loadingText ?? children}</span>
        </>
      ) : (
        <>
          {icon ? <Icon name={icon} className="shrink-0 text-[18px]" /> : null}
          {children}
        </>
      )}
    </button>
  );
});

/**
 * Submit button that reads its own pending state from the enclosing form.
 *
 * `useFormStatus` only reports the form it is rendered inside, which is exactly
 * the scoping we want — a page with three forms should not grey out all of them
 * because one is in flight.
 */
export function SubmitButton({
  children,
  loadingText = "Working…",
  ...props
}: Omit<BaseProps, "type" | "loading">) {
  const { pending } = useFormStatus();

  return (
    <Button type="submit" loading={pending} loadingText={loadingText} {...props}>
      {children}
    </Button>
  );
}

/**
 * A control whose backend does not exist yet.
 *
 * Renders exactly like the real thing and says so when clicked. This is the
 * honest option: most screens in this app are still placeholder data, and a
 * primary-looking button that does nothing at all teaches the user the product
 * is broken rather than unfinished.
 *
 * Replace with <Button> the moment the action is wired.
 */
export function StubButton({
  feature,
  children,
  onClick,
  ...props
}: BaseProps & { feature?: string }) {
  return (
    <Button
      {...props}
      onClick={(event) => {
        onClick?.(event);
        if (event.defaultPrevented) return;
        toast.notImplemented(feature ?? textOf(children) ?? "This action");
      }}
    >
      {children}
    </Button>
  );
}

/** Best-effort label for the toast when no explicit `feature` is given. */
function textOf(node: ReactNode): string | undefined {
  if (typeof node === "string") return node.trim() || undefined;
  if (typeof node === "number") return String(node);
  if (Array.isArray(node)) {
    const joined = node.map(textOf).filter(Boolean).join(" ").trim();
    return joined || undefined;
  }
  return undefined;
}

===== src/components/ui/icon.tsx =====
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * Material Symbols Outlined glyph.
 *
 * Stitch uses Material Symbols throughout rather than Lucide, so this wrapper
 * keeps the icon font usage in one place. The font is loaded in the root
 * layout. `lucide-react` remains available for anything Stitch does not cover.
 *
 * Usage mirrors the Stitch markup one-to-one:
 *   <Icon name="check_circle" filled className="text-[18px]" />
 */
export function Icon({
  name,
  filled = false,
  className,
  ...props
}: {
  /** Material Symbols ligature, e.g. "arrow_forward", "visibility_off". */
  name: string;
  /** Sets FILL 1 on the variable axis. */
  filled?: boolean;
} & React.HTMLAttributes<HTMLSpanElement>) {
  return (
    <span
      aria-hidden="true"
      className={cn("material-symbols-outlined leading-none", className)}
      style={filled ? { fontVariationSettings: "'FILL' 1" } : undefined}
      {...props}
    >
      {name}
    </span>
  );
}

===== src/components/ui/toaster.tsx =====
"use client";

import { Toaster as Sonner } from "sonner";

/**
 * Application toaster.
 *
 * Wraps sonner so the placement and theming live in one place rather than being
 * re-passed at every mount point.
 *
 * Placement is top-right at every width. The offset clears the sticky top bar
 * (h-16 on mobile, h-20 from `sm`) so a toast never lands on top of the
 * notification bell and profile avatar — the controls a user is most likely to
 * be reaching for when one appears.
 *
 * Below 600px sonner switches toasts to full-width of its container on its own,
 * so `mobileOffset` only needs to keep them off the top bar and away from the
 * screen edges.
 *
 * `closeButton` matters more than usual here: several toasts report the outcome
 * of a payroll or approval action, and a user who wants to re-read one should
 * not have to race the timeout.
 */
export function Toaster() {
  return (
    <Sonner
      richColors
      closeButton
      position="top-right"
      duration={4500}
      offset={{ top: "88px", right: "16px" }}
      mobileOffset={{ top: "72px", right: "12px", left: "12px" }}
      toastOptions={{ classNames: TOAST_CLASSNAMES }}
    />
  );
}

/**
 * Sonner's own palette is kept for the status variants (richColors) — only the
 * shell is restyled, so success/error/warning stay visually distinct instead of
 * collapsing into one brand colour.
 */
const TOAST_CLASSNAMES = {
  toast:
    "!rounded-card !border !border-outline-variant/40 !font-body-md !shadow-ambient-2",
  title: "!text-sm !font-semibold",
  description: "!text-body-md !text-on-surface-variant",
  actionButton: "!rounded-lg !bg-primary !text-on-primary !text-xs !font-semibold",
  cancelButton:
    "!rounded-lg !bg-surface-container !text-on-surface-variant !text-xs !font-semibold",
  closeButton: "!border-outline-variant/40",
};

===== src/config/env.ts =====
import "server-only";

/**
 * Validated access to server-side environment variables.
 *
 * Per the Next.js data-security guide, only the data-access layer should read
 * `process.env`. Everything else imports from here, so a missing variable
 * fails loudly the moment it's used — not at module-import time, which would
 * crash `next build`'s page-data collection for any page that transitively
 * imports this file, even pages that never render at build time.
 */
function required(name: string, value: string | undefined): string {
  if (!value) {
    throw new Error(
      `Missing required environment variable: ${name}. See .env.local.`,
    );
  }
  return value;
}

export const env = {
  /** Base URL of the Spring Boot HR/payroll API. Server-side only. */
  get API_BASE_URL(): string {
    return required("API_BASE_URL", process.env.API_BASE_URL);
  },
  /** HS256 secret for the session cookie. `openssl rand -base64 32` */
  get SESSION_SECRET(): string {
    return required("SESSION_SECRET", process.env.SESSION_SECRET);
  },
  NODE_ENV: process.env.NODE_ENV ?? "development",
} as const;

export const isProduction = env.NODE_ENV === "production";
===== src/config/navigation.ts =====
import type { Role } from "@/config/roles";

/**
 * Rail navigation per role.
 *
 * The Stitch shell is a 96px icon rail, not a 256px sidebar, so there is no
 * room for section headings — the tree is flat and the rail scrolls when a
 * role owns more routes than fit. Icons are Material Symbols ligatures
 * (rendered by <Icon />), not Lucide components, because the rail, the topbar
 * and every Stitch screen use the icon font.
 *
 * Every href sits inside the namespace that role owns (ROLE_NAMESPACE), so two
 * roles can never link to the same path.
 *
 * Menu visibility only — not an authorization boundary. The DAL re-checks on
 * every page and the Spring AccessGuard is authoritative.
 */

export type NavItem = {
  /** Rail label. Wraps to two lines at 96px, so keep it to two words. */
  title: string;
  href: string;
  /** Material Symbols Outlined ligature, e.g. "dashboard". */
  icon: string;
};
