export function navigationFor(role: Role): readonly NavItem[] {
  return ROLE_NAVIGATION[role];
}

/**
 * True when `pathname` is the item's route or sits beneath it.
 *
 * Matches on a `/` boundary so sibling routes that share a prefix stay
 * distinct — `/hr/leave-types` must not light up the `/hr/leave` item.
 */
export function isNavItemActive(pathname: string, href: string): boolean {
  return pathname === href || pathname.startsWith(`${href}/`);
}

===== src/config/roles.ts =====
/**
 * Role definitions — the single source of truth for the frontend.
 *
 * Mirrors `entity/auth/Role.java` exactly. The `users.role` column is a single
 * VARCHAR(30), so a user holds exactly one role.
 *
 * Related: security/AccessGuard, security/CustomUserDetailsService
 */

export const ROLES = [
  "SUPER_ADMIN",
  "ADMIN",
  "HR_MANAGER",
  "PAYROLL_OFFICER",
  "BRANCH_MANAGER",
  "AGENT",
  "EMPLOYEE",
  "FINANCE_OFFICER",
  "AUDITOR",
] as const;

export type Role = (typeof ROLES)[number];

export function isRole(value: unknown): value is Role {
  return typeof value === "string" && (ROLES as readonly string[]).includes(value);
}

/**
 * Which login surface a principal authenticates through.
 *
 * Four surfaces, because the credential differs per population:
 *   STAFF    /login          username + password  (users.username)
 *   EMPLOYEE /employee-login employee no. + PIN   (employees.employee_number)
 *   AGENT    /agent-login    agent code + password
 *   DEVICE   /kiosk          device token         (biometric_devices)
 */
export const PRINCIPAL_TYPES = ["STAFF", "EMPLOYEE", "AGENT", "DEVICE"] as const;
export type PrincipalType = (typeof PRINCIPAL_TYPES)[number];

export const ROLE_PRINCIPAL: Record<Role, PrincipalType> = {
  SUPER_ADMIN: "STAFF",
  ADMIN: "STAFF",
  HR_MANAGER: "STAFF",
  PAYROLL_OFFICER: "STAFF",
  BRANCH_MANAGER: "STAFF",
  AGENT: "AGENT",
  EMPLOYEE: "EMPLOYEE",
  FINANCE_OFFICER: "STAFF",
  AUDITOR: "STAFF",
};

/** Where to send an unauthenticated principal of each type. */
export const PRINCIPAL_LOGIN_ROUTE: Record<PrincipalType, string> = {
  STAFF: "/login",
  EMPLOYEE: "/employee-login",
  AGENT: "/agent-login",
  DEVICE: "/kiosk/clock",
};

/** Login route matching a known role — used when re-authenticating. */
export function loginRouteFor(role: Role): string {
  return PRINCIPAL_LOGIN_ROUTE[ROLE_PRINCIPAL[role]];
}

/**
 * URL namespace owned by each role. Every authenticated route sits under
 * exactly one prefix, so two roles can never collide on a path.
 */
export const ROLE_NAMESPACE: Record<Role, string> = {
  SUPER_ADMIN: "/admin",
  ADMIN: "/admin",
  HR_MANAGER: "/hr",
  PAYROLL_OFFICER: "/payroll",
  BRANCH_MANAGER: "/branch",
  AGENT: "/agent",
  EMPLOYEE: "/me",
  FINANCE_OFFICER: "/finance",
  AUDITOR: "/audit",
};

/** Landing route after a successful login. */
export const ROLE_HOME: Record<Role, string> = {
  SUPER_ADMIN: "/admin/dashboard",
  ADMIN: "/admin/dashboard",
  HR_MANAGER: "/hr/dashboard",
  PAYROLL_OFFICER: "/payroll/dashboard",
  BRANCH_MANAGER: "/branch/dashboard",
  AGENT: "/agent/dashboard",
  EMPLOYEE: "/me/dashboard",
  FINANCE_OFFICER: "/finance/dashboard",
  AUDITOR: "/audit/dashboard",
};

export const ROLE_LABEL: Record<Role, string> = {
  SUPER_ADMIN: "Super Admin",
  ADMIN: "Administrator",
  HR_MANAGER: "HR Manager",
  PAYROLL_OFFICER: "Payroll Officer",
  BRANCH_MANAGER: "Branch Manager",
  AGENT: "Agent",
  EMPLOYEE: "Employee",
  FINANCE_OFFICER: "Finance Officer",
  AUDITOR: "Auditor",
};

/**
 * Permissions named `<module>:<action>`, mirroring the backend module layout.
 * Suffixes narrow the data scope:
 *   :self   — only the caller's own rows
 *   :branch — rows within users.branch_id
 *   :team   — rows the caller manages
 *
 * This drives menu visibility and optimistic UI only. The Spring AccessGuard
 * stays authoritative on every request.
 */
export const PERMISSIONS = [
  "company:read", "company:write", "company:settings",
  "branch:read", "branch:read:own", "branch:write",
  "department:read", "department:write",
  "employee:read", "employee:read:branch", "employee:read:self", "employee:write",
  "employee:document:read", "employee:document:write",
  "attendance:read", "attendance:read:branch", "attendance:read:self", "attendance:write",
  "biometric:read", "biometric:enroll", "biometric:device:manage",
  "shift:read", "shift:read:branch", "shift:read:self", "shift:write", "shift:assign",
  "leave:read", "leave:read:branch", "leave:read:self",
  "leave:request", "leave:approve:manager", "leave:approve:hr", "leave:type:manage",
  "loan:read", "loan:read:self", "loan:request", "loan:approve", "loan:repayment:read",
  "agent:read", "agent:read:self", "agent:write",
  "agentadvance:read", "agentadvance:read:self", "agentadvance:request", "agentadvance:approve",
  "payroll:read", "payroll:run", "payroll:approve",
  "payslip:read", "payslip:read:self",
  "agentsummary:read", "agentsummary:read:self",
  "invoice:read", "invoice:read:self",
  "report:read", "report:read:branch", "report:read:self",
  "audit:read",
  "notification:read:self",
  "user:manage",
  "settings:manage",
] as const;

export type Permission = (typeof PERMISSIONS)[number];

export const ROLE_PERMISSIONS: Record<Role, readonly Permission[]> = {
  /** Platform owner: multi-company, system config, no day-to-day HR. */
  SUPER_ADMIN: [
    "company:read", "company:write", "company:settings",
    "branch:read", "branch:write",
    "department:read", "department:write",
    // Attendance read is here because the platform owner owns the
    // company-wide attendance board at /admin/attendance and the backend's
    // `AttendanceDashboardController.VIEW_ROLES` includes SUPER_ADMIN. Without
    // it the DAL gate bounces SUPER_ADMIN off a page built for them.
    "attendance:read",
    // The same reasoning covers these two reads, and both were omissions
    // rather than policy. `/admin/agents` is in the platform owner's rail and
    // the backend's `GET /agents` is
    // `hasAnyRole('SUPER_ADMIN', 'ADMIN', 'PAYROLL_OFFICER')` — omitting the
    // permission here bounced SUPER_ADMIN off a page the API serves them. The
    // /admin dashboard's headcount tile reads
    // `GET /employees/by-branch/{id}`, whose `@PreAuthorize` likewise lists
    // SUPER_ADMIN.
    //
    // Reads only: the `:write` counterparts stay out, so the "no day-to-day HR"
    // intent above still holds — oversight without operational control.
    "agent:read",
    "employee:read",
    "biometric:device:manage",

    "notification:read:self",

  ADMIN: [

  AUDITOR: [
    "company:read", "branch:read", "department:read",
    "employee:read", "attendance:read", "shift:read",
    "leave:read", "loan:read", "loan:repayment:read",
    "agent:read", "agentadvance:read",
    "payroll:read", "payslip:read", "agentsummary:read", "invoice:read",
    "report:read", "audit:read",
    "notification:read:self",
  ],
};

export function hasPermission(role: Role, permission: Permission): boolean {
  return ROLE_PERMISSIONS[role].includes(permission);
}

export function hasAnyPermission(
  role: Role,
  permissions: readonly Permission[],
): boolean {
  return permissions.some((p) => hasPermission(role, p));
}

/** True when `pathname` falls inside the namespace this role owns. */
export function canAccessPath(role: Role, pathname: string): boolean {
  const namespace = ROLE_NAMESPACE[role];
  return pathname === namespace || pathname.startsWith(`${namespace}/`);
}

===== src/lib/brand-icon.tsx =====
import { readFile } from "node:fs/promises";
import path from "node:path";

import { ImageResponse } from "next/og";

/**
 * App-icon rendering for the `icon` / `apple-icon` file conventions.
 *
 * The brand asset is one JPEG containing the emblem above the wordmark, and at
 * favicon sizes the wordmark is noise — so this crops to the emblem alone, the
 * same crop `BrandMark` does in CSS. The two are separate implementations of
 * one intent (Satori cannot reuse Tailwind classes), so the box below must stay
 * in step with the values documented in `components/shared/brand-logo.tsx`.
 */
const SOURCE_ASPECT = 673 / 684;

/** Emblem bounding box as fractions of the source image. */
const EMBLEM_LEFT = 0.07;
const EMBLEM_RIGHT = 0.975;
const EMBLEM_TOP = 0.025;
const EMBLEM_BOTTOM = 0.655;

/**
 * Renders the emblem, scaled to span a square icon's full width and centred on
 * its height. Read from disk at request time rather than imported so the JPEG
 * stays out of the route bundle.
 */
export async function renderBrandIcon(edge: number) {
  const bytes = await readFile(
    path.join(process.cwd(), "public", "brand", "dhwarsh-logo.jpg"),
  );
  const source = `data:image/jpeg;base64,${bytes.toString("base64")}`;

  const width = edge / (EMBLEM_RIGHT - EMBLEM_LEFT);
  const height = width * SOURCE_ASPECT;
  const emblemHeight = (EMBLEM_BOTTOM - EMBLEM_TOP) * height;

  return new ImageResponse(
    (
      <div
        style={{
          position: "relative",
          display: "flex",
          width: edge,
          height: edge,
          overflow: "hidden",
          backgroundColor: "#ffffff",
        }}
      >
        {/* eslint-disable-next-line @next/next/no-img-element --
            Satori renders plain <img>; next/image has no meaning here. */}
        <img
          alt=""
          src={source}
          width={width}
          height={height}
          style={{
            position: "absolute",
            left: -EMBLEM_LEFT * width,
            top: (edge - emblemHeight) / 2 - EMBLEM_TOP * height,
          }}
        />
      </div>
    ),
    { width: edge, height: edge },
  );
}

===== src/lib/format.ts =====
import { format, formatDistanceToNowStrict, parseISO } from "date-fns";

/** Display formatting. Never used for computation — the payroll engine owns that. */

const DATE_INPUT = "yyyy-MM-dd";

function safeParse(value: string | null | undefined): Date | null {
  if (!value) return null;
  try {
    const parsed = parseISO(value);
    return Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime()) ? null : parsed;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

/** "12 Aug 2026" */
export function formatDate(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  const date = safeParse(value);
  return date ? format(date, "dd MMM yyyy") : "—";
}

/** "12 Aug 2026, 14:30" */
export function formatDateTime(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  const date = safeParse(value);
  return date ? format(date, "dd MMM yyyy, HH:mm") : "—";
}

/** "14:30" — for clock-in/out columns. */
export function formatTime(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  if (!value) return "—";
  const date = safeParse(value);
  if (date) return format(date, "HH:mm");
  // Bare TIME column, e.g. "08:00:00"
  const match = /^(\d{2}):(\d{2})/.exec(value);
  return match ? `${match[1]}:${match[2]}` : "—";
}

/**
 * "12 minutes ago" — freshness, not a timestamp.
 *
 * Only for liveness signals where "how stale is this" is the whole point: a
 * device heartbeat, a sync cursor. Anything that has to reconcile later — an
 * audit row, a clock-in, a payslip date — uses `formatDateTime`, because this
 * output changes on every render and cannot be read back.
 */
export function formatRelativeTime(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  const date = safeParse(value);
  return date ? formatDistanceToNowStrict(date, { addSuffix: true }) : "—";
}

/** "August 2026" — payslip/payroll period heading. */
export function formatMonthYear(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  const date = safeParse(value);
  return date ? format(date, "MMMM yyyy") : "—";
}

/** Value for an <input type="date">. */
export function toDateInput(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  const date = safeParse(value);
  return date ? format(date, DATE_INPUT) : "";
}

/** "1 Aug – 31 Aug 2026" — payroll period display. */
export function formatDateRange(
  start: string | null | undefined,
  end: string | null | undefined,
): string {
  const from = safeParse(start);
  const to = safeParse(end);
  if (!from || !to) return "—";

  const sameYear = from.getFullYear() === to.getFullYear();
  const left = format(from, sameYear ? "d MMM" : "d MMM yyyy");
  return `${left} – ${format(to, "d MMM yyyy")}`;
}

/**
 * "KES 125,000.00". Defaults to KES to match `companies.currency`; pass the
 * company's configured currency where one is available.
 *
 * `showCurrency: false` drops the symbol but keeps the two decimal places —
 * for money columns where the currency sits in the header and repeating it on
 * every row is noise. The figure still reconciles to the cent.
 */
export function formatMoney(
  amount: number | null | undefined,
  { currency = "KES", showCurrency = true }: {
    currency?: string;
    showCurrency?: boolean;
  } = {},
): string {
  if (amount === null || amount === undefined) return "—";

  return new Intl.NumberFormat("en-KE", {
    style: showCurrency ? "currency" : "decimal",
    currency,
    minimumFractionDigits: 2,
    maximumFractionDigits: 2,
  }).format(amount);
}

/** "125,000" — compact form for KPI tiles. */
export function formatNumber(value: number | null | undefined): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined) return "—";
  return new Intl.NumberFormat("en-KE").format(value);
}

/**
 * "KES 18.4M" — dashboard tiles, where the exact shilling is noise and the
 * column is too narrow for the full figure.
 *
 * Never use this on a payslip, invoice or disbursement total: those must
 * reconcile against the backend to the cent. Use `formatMoney` there.
 */
export function formatMoneyCompact(
  amount: number | null | undefined,
  { currency = "KES", showCurrency = true }: {
    currency?: string;
    showCurrency?: boolean;
  } = {},
): string {
  if (amount === null || amount === undefined) return "—";

  const compact = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-KE", {
    notation: "compact",
    maximumFractionDigits: 1,
  }).format(amount);

  return showCurrency ? `${currency} ${compact}` : compact;
}

/** "16%" — VAT rate from company_settings. */
export function formatPercent(
  value: number | null | undefined,
  fractionDigits = 0,
): string {
  if (value === null || value === undefined) return "—";
  return `${value.toFixed(fractionDigits)}%`;
}

/** "DK" — avatar fallback. */
export function initials(name: string | null | undefined): string {
  if (!name?.trim()) return "?";
  const parts = name.trim().split(/\s+/);
  const first = parts[0]?.[0] ?? "";
  const last = parts.length > 1 ? (parts[parts.length - 1]?.[0] ?? "") : "";
  return (first + last).toUpperCase();
}

/** "PENDING_HR" → "Pending HR" */
export function humanizeEnum(value: string | null | undefined): string {
  if (!value) return "—";
  return value
    .split("_")
    .map((word) =>
      word === "HR" || word === "VAT" || word === "PAYE"
        ? word
        : word.charAt(0) + word.slice(1).toLowerCase(),
    )
    .join(" ");
}

/** Hours worked between clock-in and clock-out, e.g. "8h 15m". */
export function formatDuration(minutes: number | null | undefined): string {
  if (minutes === null || minutes === undefined) return "—";
  const hours = Math.floor(minutes / 60);
  const mins = Math.round(minutes % 60);
  if (hours === 0) return `${mins}m`;
  return mins === 0 ? `${hours}h` : `${hours}h ${mins}m`;
}

/**
 * "8h 15m" from a decimal number of hours.
 *
 * The attendance dashboard reports `hoursWorked` as hours (8.25), while
 * `formatDuration` counts in minutes. Converting at the call site is the kind
 * of unit mix-up that silently renders 8 minutes as "0h 8m", so the conversion
 * lives here next to the function it feeds.
 */
export function formatHours(hours: number | null | undefined): string {
  if (hours === null || hours === undefined) return "—";
  return formatDuration(Math.round(hours * 60));
}

===== src/lib/search-params.ts =====
/**
 * Server-side search-param helpers for portal pages.
 *
 * `searchParams` is a Promise in Next.js 16 and every value arrives as
 * `string | string[] | undefined`. These lift the parsing each list page
 * would otherwise repeat verbatim — the originals were private helpers in
 * `app/(portal)/admin/attendance/page.tsx`.
 *
 * Parsing is deliberately strict: anything that is not a well-formed value is
 * treated as absent rather than coerced, because a bad `branchId` silently
 * becoming `NaN` produces an empty table that looks like a data problem.
 */

/** Collapses Next's `string | string[] | undefined` to a single value. */
export function first(
  value: string | string[] | undefined,
): string | undefined {
  return Array.isArray(value) ? value[0] : value;
}

/** A positive integer id, or `undefined` if absent/malformed. */
export function parseId(value: string | undefined): number | undefined {
  if (!value) return undefined;
  const parsed = Number(value);
  return Number.isInteger(parsed) && parsed > 0 ? parsed : undefined;
}

/** `yyyy-MM-dd` only. Parametric date filters reject anything else with a 400. */
export function parseDate(value: string | undefined): string | undefined {
  return value && /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(value) ? value : undefined;
}

/** Zero-based page index. The API is 0-based; the UI shows `pageNumber + 1`. */
export function parsePage(value: string | undefined): number {
  const parsed = Number(value);
  return Number.isInteger(parsed) && parsed > 0 ? parsed : 0;
}

/** Page size, clamped to what the backend will accept. */
export function parseSize(
  value: string | undefined,
  fallback: number,
  max = 100,
): number {
  const parsed = Number(value);
  if (!Number.isInteger(parsed) || parsed <= 0) return fallback;
  return Math.min(parsed, max);
}

/** A free-text query, trimmed, with empty strings read as absent. */
export function parseQuery(value: string | undefined): string | undefined {
  const trimmed = value?.trim();
  return trimmed ? trimmed : undefined;
}

/**
 * Rebuilds a URL for one of `basePath`'s pages, preserving the active
 * filters and dropping the ones that are unset.
 *
 * Page 0 is omitted so the canonical list URL stays clean.
 */
export function buildHref(
  basePath: string,
  params: Record<string, string | number | undefined>,
  page?: number,
): string {
  const search = new URLSearchParams();

  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(params)) {
    if (value !== undefined && value !== "") search.set(key, String(value));
  }

  if (page !== undefined && page > 0) search.set("page", String(page));

  const query = search.toString();
  return query ? `${basePath}?${query}` : basePath;
}

===== src/lib/toast.ts =====
"use client";

import { toast as sonner } from "sonner";

/**
 * Typed toast helpers.
 *
 * Everything user-facing goes through here rather than calling sonner directly,
 * so copy stays consistent and the "nothing happened" failure mode has one
 * place to be fixed. Feedback for an HR action is not decoration: an approval
 * that silently succeeds and an approval that silently fails look identical,
 * and the user's next move differs completely.
 *
 * Messages are sentence-case and say what happened to *what*, not just that
 * something happened — "Leave request approved" beats "Success".
 */

type ToastOptions = {
  description?: string;
  /** Milliseconds. Defaults to the Toaster's own duration. */
  duration?: number;
  /**
   * Primary affordance on the toast. Use for undo on an optimistic change —
   * it is the difference between a mis-tap costing a keystroke and costing a
   * page reload.
   */
  action?: { label: string; onClick: () => void };
};

export const toast = {
  success(message: string, options?: ToastOptions) {
    return sonner.success(message, options);
  },

  error(message: string, options?: ToastOptions) {
    // Errors hang around longer — the user usually has to act on them, and a
    // failure that vanishes before it is read is worse than no toast at all.
    return sonner.error(message, { duration: 7000, ...options });
  },

  info(message: string, options?: ToastOptions) {
    return sonner.info(message, options);
  },

  warning(message: string, options?: ToastOptions) {
    // A toast carrying an Undo has to outlive the user's "wait, what did I just
    // click?" pause, so it gets the long duration rather than the default.
    const duration = options?.action ? 10000 : 6000;
    return sonner.warning(message, { duration, ...options });
  },

  /**
   * Pending → settled, driven by a promise. Use for anything that leaves the
   * browser, so a slow network reads as "working" rather than "broken".
   */
  promise<T>(
    promise: Promise<T>,
    messages: {
      loading: string;
      success: string | ((data: T) => string);
      error: string | ((error: unknown) => string);
    },
  ) {
    return sonner.promise(promise, messages);
  },

  /**
   * Honest feedback for a control whose backend does not exist yet.
   *
   * Most screens in this app are still placeholder data. A button that looks
   * live, is clicked, and does nothing teaches the user the app is broken; one
   * that says the feature is not connected yet teaches them to wait. Neither is
   * a working feature, but only one of them is a lie.
   */
  notImplemented(feature: string) {
    return sonner.info(`${feature} isn't connected yet`, {
      description: "This screen is running on placeholder data until the API is wired up.",
      duration: 4000,
    });
  },

  dismiss(id?: string | number) {
    return sonner.dismiss(id);
  },
};

===== src/lib/api/agent.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type { AgentResponse, EmployeeSummaryResponse } from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/agent/AgentController` — base `/agents`.
 *
 * All three reads return bare `ApiResponse<List<…>>`, not a page: the backend
 * loads the whole agent table and there is no `GET /agents` paging. That is
 * fine for a labour-supplier list in the tens; if it ever grows past a few
 * hundred this module is where paging gets added, and the page that calls it
 * will need a `Paginated<T>` in place of the array.
 */

/** Every agent. Open to SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN and PAYROLL_OFFICER. */
export function getAgents(signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<AgentResponse[]> {
  return api.get<AgentResponse[]>("/agents", { signal });
}

/** One agent. Also open to PAYROLL_OFFICER and to the agent itself. */
export function getAgent(
  id: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<AgentResponse> {
  return api.get<AgentResponse>(`/agents/${id}`, { signal });
}

/** The signed-in agent's own profile. AGENT principal only. */
export function getMyAgent(signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<AgentResponse> {
  return api.get<AgentResponse>("/agents/me", { signal });
}

/** Staff deployed by one agent. */
export function getAgentStaff(
  agentId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]> {
  return api.get<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]>(`/employees/by-agent/${agentId}`, {
    signal,
  });
}

/**
 * Deactivates an agent.
 *
 * SUPER_ADMIN only — `AgentController.deactivateAgent` is `hasRole('SUPER_ADMIN')`,
 * while `createAgent`/`updateAgent` accept ADMIN too. The UI must therefore hide
 * this control for ADMIN rather than render it and let the request 403.
 */
export function deactivateAgent(id: number): Promise<void> {
  return api.put<void>(`/agents/${id}/deactivate`);
}

===== src/lib/api/attendance.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type {
  AttendanceActivityItem,
  AttendanceDashboardSummary,
  AttendanceDeviceItem,
  AttendanceRecordItem,
  AttendanceResponse,
  AttendanceSyncFailure,
  Paginated,
} from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/attendance/AttendanceDashboardController`.
 *
 * Every function here is a read. They live in one module rather than inline at
 * the call sites because two callers need the same endpoints: the page itself
 * and the polling route at `app/api/attendance/live`. Duplicating the paths
 * would mean a rename on the backend breaks one of them silently.
 *
 * There are no write endpoints in this module on purpose — the dashboard is
 * read-only. Manual attendance corrections live on the HR surfaces.
 */

export type AttendanceSummaryParams = {
  /** Business day to summarise. Defaults to today (company timezone). */
  date?: string;
  branchId?: number;
};

export type AttendanceActivityParams = {
  branchId?: number;
  /** ISO date-time. Omit for "since the start of today". */
  since?: string;
  /** Backend clamps to 1..200. */
  limit?: number;
};

export type AttendanceRecordsParams = {
  branchId?: number;
  /** Inclusive, `yyyy-MM-dd`. Defaults to today. */
  start?: string;
  end?: string;
  /** Only employees still on shift. */
  openOnly?: boolean;
  page?: number;
  size?: number;
};

/** Headcount, clock-in counts, attendance rate and per-branch roll-up. */
export function getAttendanceSummary(
  params: AttendanceSummaryParams = {},
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<AttendanceDashboardSummary> {
  return api.get<AttendanceDashboardSummary>("/attendance/dashboard/summary", {
    query: { date: params.date, branchId: params.branchId },
    signal,
  });
}

/** The live feed, newest first. Each record contributes up to two entries. */
export function getAttendanceActivity(
  params: AttendanceActivityParams = {},
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<AttendanceActivityItem[]> {
  return api.get<AttendanceActivityItem[]>("/attendance/dashboard/activity", {
    query: {
      branchId: params.branchId,
      since: params.since,
      limit: params.limit ?? 50,
    },
    signal,
  });
}

/**
 * The records table. The backend returns its own `PageResponse<T>`, whose
 * fields match the frontend's `Paginated<T>` one for one — so `getPage` is
 * right here and `get` would be wrong.
 */
export function getAttendanceRecords(
  params: AttendanceRecordsParams = {},
): Promise<Paginated<AttendanceRecordItem>> {
  return api.getPage<AttendanceRecordItem>("/attendance/dashboard/records", {
    query: {
      branchId: params.branchId,
      start: params.start,
      end: params.end,
      openOnly: params.openOnly,
      page: params.page ?? 0,
      size: params.size ?? 25,
    },
  });
}

/**
 * The terminal fleet. `online` is computed server-side against a 5-minute
 * freshness window, so it is a real liveness signal and not a stored flag.
 */
export function getAttendanceDevices(
  params: { branchId?: number } = {},
): Promise<AttendanceDeviceItem[]> {
  return api.get<AttendanceDeviceItem[]>("/attendance/dashboard/devices", {
    query: { branchId: params.branchId },
  });
}

/**
 * Scans the backend refused, last 100. This is the only surface where an
 * administrator learns that a clock-in silently did not happen — a rejected
 * event leaves no row in `attendance`.
 */
export function getAttendanceSyncFailures(): Promise<AttendanceSyncFailure[]> {
  return api.get<AttendanceSyncFailure[]>(
    "/attendance/dashboard/sync-failures",
  );
}

/**
 * One employee's attendance over a date window.
 *
 * `start` and `end` are `@RequestParam` on the backend and are **required** —
 * omitting either is a 400, not a defaulted range. Both are inclusive
 * `yyyy-MM-dd`.
 */
export function getAttendanceForEmployee(
  employeeId: number,
  params: { start: string; end: string },
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<AttendanceResponse[]> {
  return api.get<AttendanceResponse[]>(`/attendance/employee/${employeeId}`, {
    query: { start: params.start, end: params.end },
    signal,
  });
}

/** One branch's attendance over a date window. `start` and `end` required. */
export function getAttendanceForBranch(
  branchId: number,
  params: { start: string; end: string },
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<AttendanceResponse[]> {
  return api.get<AttendanceResponse[]>(`/attendance/branch/${branchId}`, {
    query: { start: params.start, end: params.end },
    signal,
  });
}

===== src/lib/api/department.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type { DepartmentResponse } from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/department/DepartmentController` — base `/departments`.
 *
 * There is deliberately no `getDepartments()`: the backend has no
 * `GET /departments` list endpoint. Departments are only reachable through a
 * branch (`by-branch`) or by id. A flat company-wide department list is a
 * backend gap, not an oversight here — adding it would mean a new controller
 * method, which is outside what the current API can serve.
 */

/** Every department in one branch. */
export function getDepartmentsByBranch(
  branchId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<DepartmentResponse[]> {
  return api.get<DepartmentResponse[]>(`/departments/by-branch/${branchId}`, {
    signal,
  });
}

/** One department. */
export function getDepartment(
  id: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<DepartmentResponse> {
  return api.get<DepartmentResponse>(`/departments/${id}`, { signal });
}

/** Deletes a department. SUPER_ADMIN and ADMIN — **not** HR_MANAGER. */
export function deleteDepartment(id: number): Promise<void> {
  return api.delete<void>(`/departments/${id}`);
}

/** Payload for create and update — the backend has one `DepartmentRequest`. */
export type DepartmentInput = {
  name: string;
  branchId: number;
  headOfDepartmentId?: number;
};

/** Creates a department. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN and HR_MANAGER. */
export function createDepartment(
  payload: DepartmentInput,
): Promise<DepartmentResponse> {
  return api.post<DepartmentResponse>("/departments", payload);
}

/**
 * Updates a department. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN and HR_MANAGER.
 *
 * `branchId` is part of the payload, not optional: moving a department to
 * another branch is what this endpoint does, and the service re-checks that any
 * employee still pointing at it stays consistent.
 */
export function updateDepartment(
  id: number,
  payload: DepartmentInput,
): Promise<DepartmentResponse> {

===== src/lib/api/document.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type { EmployeeDocumentResponse } from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/employee/EmployeeDocumentController` — base
 * `/employees/{employeeId}/documents`.
 *
 * The upload is `multipart/form-data`, not JSON: `documentType` travels as a
 * request *param* alongside the file part rather than in a JSON body, because
 * Spring cannot bind a JSON body and a MultipartFile on the same request.
 *
 * `api.post` detects a `FormData` body and deliberately leaves Content-Type
 * unset, so the runtime can add the multipart boundary — setting it by hand
 * would break the parse.
 *
 * `fileUrl` on the response is a server-side path, not a URL this app can
 * render in an <img> or link to; there is no download endpoint for documents.
 */

/** Documents attached to one employee. */
export function getEmployeeDocuments(
  employeeId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeDocumentResponse[]> {
  return api.get<EmployeeDocumentResponse[]>(
    `/employees/${employeeId}/documents`,
    { signal },
  );
}

/**
 * Uploads one document. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN, HR_MANAGER.
 *
 * `documentType` is a free-form `String` on the backend — there is no
 * `DocumentType` enum and no validation, only a comment on the entity listing
 * CONTRACT, ID, CERTIFICATE, OTHER. So nothing here can 400 on the type, but a
 * value outside that set makes the column's own documentation wrong. `@/types`
 * `DOCUMENT_TYPES` is the vocabulary this app writes; it is a superset of the
 * entity's comment (it has NATIONAL_ID rather than ID, plus KRA_PIN and CV).
 */
export function uploadEmployeeDocument(
  employeeId: number,
  documentType: string,
  file: File,
): Promise<EmployeeDocumentResponse> {
  const body = new FormData();
  body.set("documentType", documentType);
  body.set("file", file);

  return api.post<EmployeeDocumentResponse>(
    `/employees/${employeeId}/documents`,
    body,
  );
}

/** Removes a document. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN, HR_MANAGER. */
export function deleteEmployeeDocument(
  employeeId: number,
  documentId: number,
): Promise<void> {
  return api.delete<void>(`/employees/${employeeId}/documents/${documentId}`);
}

===== src/lib/api/employee.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type { EmployeeInput } from "@/app/lib/definitions";
import type { EmployeeResponse, EmployeeSummaryResponse } from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/employee/EmployeeController` — base `/employees`.
 *
 * There is no `GET /employees` list: the backend only exposes employees
 * through a scope — by branch, by agent, by department, or `/me`. A
 * company-wide employee directory therefore has to be assembled branch by
 * branch. That is a real limitation of the current API, and the reason the
 * `/hr/employees` list cannot be a single call.
 *
 * Note the asymmetry between the two shapes: every `by-*` endpoint returns the
 * thin `EmployeeSummaryResponse`, and only `/{id}` and `/me` return the full
 * `EmployeeResponse`. Salary and statutory identifiers never appear in a list.
 */

/** Employees in one branch. */
export function getEmployeesByBranch(
  branchId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]> {
  return api.get<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]>(
    `/employees/by-branch/${branchId}`,
    { signal },
  );
}

/** Staff an agent has deployed. */
export function getEmployeesByAgent(
  agentId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]> {
  return api.get<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]>(`/employees/by-agent/${agentId}`, {
    signal,
  });
}

/** Employees in one department. */
export function getEmployeesByDepartment(
  departmentId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]> {
  return api.get<EmployeeSummaryResponse[]>(
    `/employees/by-department/${departmentId}`,
    { signal },
  );
}

/** The full record for one employee. */
export function getEmployee(
  id: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeResponse> {
  return api.get<EmployeeResponse>(`/employees/${id}`, { signal });
}

/** The signed-in employee's own record. EMPLOYEE principal only. */
export function getMyEmployeeProfile(
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<EmployeeResponse> {
  return api.get<EmployeeResponse>("/employees/me", { signal });
}

/** Terminates an employee. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN and HR_MANAGER. */
export function terminateEmployee(id: number): Promise<void> {
  return api.put<void>(`/employees/${id}/terminate`);
}

/**
 * Creates an employee. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN and HR_MANAGER.
 *
 * The payload is `EmployeeRequest`, which is also what `updateEmployee` takes —
 * there is one DTO, not a create/update pair.
 *
 * Two things the server owns and the caller must not send: `employeeNumber`,
 * generated as `EMP-<branchCode>-<seq>` inside the service, and
 * `employmentStatus`, which is not a field on the request at all.
 *
 * Failure modes the form has to distinguish:
 *   409  a duplicate `nationalId` (the column is UNIQUE) or employee number
 *   404  an unknown `branchId`, `departmentId` or `agentId`
 *   400  a `departmentId` that does not belong to `branchId` — the service
 *        raises InvalidOperationException for that
 */
export function createEmployee(payload: EmployeeInput): Promise<EmployeeResponse> {
  return api.post<EmployeeResponse>("/employees", payload);
}

/** Updates an employee. Same DTO and roles as create. */
export function updateEmployee(
  id: number,
  payload: EmployeeInput,
): Promise<EmployeeResponse> {
  return api.put<EmployeeResponse>(`/employees/${id}`, payload);
}

===== src/lib/api/leave.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type {
  LeaveBalanceResponse,
  LeaveRequestResponse,
  LeaveTypeResponse,
} from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/leave/LeaveController` — base `/leave`.
 *
 * HR's slice of this controller is the final approval stage and the per-employee
 * history:
 *
 *   GET  /leave/types                      no @PreAuthorize — any principal
 *   GET  /leave/pending/hr                 HR_MANAGER, SUPER_ADMIN
 *   GET  /leave/employee/{employeeId}      SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN, HR_MANAGER
 *   PUT  /leave/{id}/approve-by-hr         HR_MANAGER, SUPER_ADMIN
 *
 * Two absences worth knowing about, because they shape the screens:
 *
 * - There is **no `GET /leave` list** and **no `GET /leave/{id}`**. The only
 *   company-wide view is `/pending/hr`, which is by definition one stage of one
 *   status. An "all leave requests" table cannot be built from this API, and
 *   `/hr/leave/[id]` has no source to read from.
 * - `applyForLeave` and `/my-balance` are `hasRole('EMPLOYEE')`, and
 *   `approve-by-manager` is `hasRole('BRANCH_MANAGER')` — they belong to other
 *   rails and are deliberately not wrapped here.
 */

/** Every configured leave type. Unauthenticated-by-role, so always available. */
export function getLeaveTypes(
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<LeaveTypeResponse[]> {
  return api.get<LeaveTypeResponse[]>("/leave/types", { signal });
}

/**
 * Requests that have cleared the branch manager and await HR's decision.
 *
 * This is the HR approval queue. It returns only the requests sitting at
 * `PENDING_HR` — approving or rejecting one removes it from the next read.
 */
export function getPendingHrApprovals(
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<LeaveRequestResponse[]> {
  return api.get<LeaveRequestResponse[]>("/leave/pending/hr", { signal });
}

/** One employee's leave history, newest first. */
export function getLeaveHistoryForEmployee(
  employeeId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<LeaveRequestResponse[]> {
  return api.get<LeaveRequestResponse[]>(`/leave/employee/${employeeId}`, {
    signal,
  });
}

/**
 * HR's final decision on a request.
 *
 * Rejection goes through this same call with `approved: false` — there is no
 * separate reject endpoint. `rejectionReason` is only meaningful when rejecting,
 * and the backend does not require it.
 *
 * On approval the controller publishes a `LeaveApprovedEvent` addressed to the
 * employee's user account. If the employee has no account yet (onboarding not
 * finished) it logs a warning and sends nothing — the approval still stands.
 */
export function decideLeaveByHr(
  leaveRequestId: number,
  decision: { approved: boolean; rejectionReason?: string },
): Promise<LeaveRequestResponse> {
  return api.put<LeaveRequestResponse>(
    `/leave/${leaveRequestId}/approve-by-hr`,
    decision,
  );
}

/**
 * Leave balances for one employee.
 *
 * Backed by `GET /leave/my-balance`, which is `hasRole('EMPLOYEE')` — HR cannot
 * read someone else's balance through it. Kept here only so the EMPLOYEE rail
 * has one import site; HR screens must not call it.
 */
export function getMyLeaveBalances(
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<LeaveBalanceResponse[]> {
  return api.get<LeaveBalanceResponse[]>("/leave/my-balance", { signal });
}

===== src/lib/api/query-keys.ts =====
/**
 * React Query cache keys, one namespace per backend module.
 *
 * Mirrors the `event/` package: when a domain event fires server-side, the
 * matching key below is what the client invalidates. Keeping them in one file
 * makes "what goes stale when payroll completes?" answerable by reading, not
 * grepping.
 */

export const queryKeys = {
  auth: {
    currentUser: ["auth", "current-user"] as const,
  },

  company: {
    all: ["company"] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["company", id] as const,
    settings: (id: number) => ["company", id, "settings"] as const,
  },

  branch: {
    all: ["branch"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["branch", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["branch", id] as const,
  },

  department: {
    all: ["department"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["department", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["department", id] as const,
  },

  employee: {
    all: ["employee"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["employee", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["employee", id] as const,
    documents: (id: number) => ["employee", id, "documents"] as const,
  },

  attendance: {
    all: ["attendance"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["attendance", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    byEmployee: (employeeId: number, filters?: unknown) =>
      ["attendance", "employee", employeeId, filters ?? {}] as const,
    /**
     * Polled snapshot behind the live board. Keyed by branch because switching
     * the filter must refetch, not serve the previous branch's feed.
     * See `app/api/attendance/live/route.ts`.
     */
    live: (branchId?: number) =>
      ["attendance", "live", branchId ?? "all"] as const,
  },

  biometric: {
    devices: ["biometric", "devices"] as const,
    templates: (employeeId: number) =>
      ["biometric", "templates", employeeId] as const,
  },

  shift: {
    all: ["shift"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["shift", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    assignments: (filters?: unknown) =>
      ["shift", "assignments", filters ?? {}] as const,
  },

  leave: {
    all: ["leave"] as const,
    types: ["leave", "types"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["leave", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["leave", id] as const,
    pendingApprovals: ["leave", "pending-approvals"] as const,
  },

  loan: {
    all: ["loan"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["loan", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["loan", id] as const,
    repayments: (loanId: number) => ["loan", loanId, "repayments"] as const,
  },

  agent: {
    all: ["agent"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["agent", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["agent", id] as const,
    staff: (id: number) => ["agent", id, "staff"] as const,
  },

  agentAdvance: {
    all: ["agent-advance"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) =>
      ["agent-advance", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
    detail: (id: number) => ["agent-advance", id] as const,
  },

  payroll: {
    all: ["payroll"] as const,
    runs: (filters?: unknown) => ["payroll", "runs", filters ?? {}] as const,
    run: (id: number) => ["payroll", "run", id] as const,
    /** Polled while a run is PROCESSING. */
    runStatus: (id: number) => ["payroll", "run", id, "status"] as const,
    payslips: (runId: number) => ["payroll", "run", runId, "payslips"] as const,
    payslip: (id: number) => ["payroll", "payslip", id] as const,
    agentSummaries: (runId: number) =>
      ["payroll", "run", runId, "agent-summaries"] as const,
  },

  notification: {
    all: ["notification"] as const,
    unreadCount: ["notification", "unread-count"] as const,
  },

  audit: {
    all: ["audit"] as const,
    list: (filters?: unknown) => ["audit", "list", filters ?? {}] as const,
  },

  report: {
    all: ["report"] as const,
    dashboard: (role: string) => ["report", "dashboard", role] as const,
  },
} as const;

/**
 * Cache tags for `revalidateTag` in Server Actions. Kept parallel to the keys
 * above so a mutation can bust both layers consistently.
 */
export const cacheTags = {
  employees: "employees",
  branches: "branches",
  departments: "departments",
  agents: "agents",
  agentAdvances: "agent-advances",
  attendance: "attendance",
  shifts: "shifts",
  leave: "leave",
  loans: "loans",
  payrollRuns: "payroll-runs",
  payslips: "payslips",
  notifications: "notifications",
  auditLogs: "audit-logs",
} as const;

===== src/lib/api/report.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { fetchDocument } from "@/app/lib/api-client";

/**
 * `controller/report/ReportController` — base `/reports`.
 *
 * All three of these return a PDF, not JSON: the controller writes
 * `ResponseEntity<byte[]>` with `Content-Type: application/pdf` and
 * `Content-Disposition: attachment`, deliberately bypassing the
 * `ApiResponse<T>` envelope (wrapping a file's bytes in JSON would corrupt it).
 * So they go through `fetchDocument` rather than `api.get`, and the result is a
 * `Blob` plus the filename the backend chose.
 *
 * Every function here is `hasAnyRole('SUPER_ADMIN', 'ADMIN', 'HR_MANAGER',
 * 'PAYROLL_OFFICER', 'FINANCE_OFFICER', 'AUDITOR')` on the Spring side, and
 * `attendance/{branchId}` additionally admits a BRANCH_MANAGER on their own
 * branch. There is no report an EMPLOYEE or AGENT may generate.
 *
 * These are called from a Route Handler, never from a page: a Server Component
 * cannot hand a `Blob` to the browser, and the bytes must not pass through the
 * RSC payload.
 */

/** Clock activity for one branch over a range. Both bounds are required. */
export function getAttendanceReport(
  branchId: number,
  params: { start: string; end: string },
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<{ blob: Blob; filename: string }> {
  return fetchDocument(`/reports/attendance/${branchId}`, {
    query: { start: params.start, end: params.end },
    signal,
  });
}

/**
 * The register for one completed payroll run.
 *
 * HR may generate this but has no way to *find* a run id: `GET /payroll/runs`
 * does not exist and every `/payroll/runs/{id}` read is restricted to
 * SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN, PAYROLL_OFFICER, FINANCE_OFFICER and AUDITOR — HR_MANAGER
 * is not in that list. The id has to be supplied from outside.
 */
export function getPayrollRegisterReport(
  payrollRunId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<{ blob: Blob; filename: string }> {
  return fetchDocument(`/reports/payroll-register/${payrollRunId}`, { signal });
}

/**
 * The employee directory as a PDF, for one or more branches.
 *
 * `branchIds` goes on the wire as a repeated query param. Spring binds both
 * that and the comma-separated form the controller's own comment describes to
 * `List<Long>`, and repeating the key is what `buildUrl` does natively.
 */
export function getEmployeeListReport(
  branchIds: number[],
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<{ blob: Blob; filename: string }> {
  return fetchDocument("/reports/employee-list", {
    query: { branchIds },
    signal,
  });
}

===== src/lib/api/shift.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type {
  ShiftAssignmentResponse,
  ShiftResponse,
} from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/shift/ShiftController` — base `/shifts`.
 *
 * HR has the full surface here, which makes shifts the one HR module with no
 * permission caveats: create, update, list, assign, and read assignments by
 * employee or branch.
 *
 * There is no `GET /shifts/{id}` and no delete — a shift is never removed, only
 * edited, because assignments reference it.
 *
 * `ShiftRequest` is the same record for create and update.
 */

export type ShiftInput = {
  name: string;
  /** `entity/shift/ShiftType` — MORNING, EVENING, NIGHT, ROTATING, FLEXIBLE. */
  type: string;
  /** `LocalTime`, serialised "HH:mm" or "HH:mm:ss". Omit for none. */
  startTime?: string;
  endTime?: string;
};

/**
 * A shift assignment. The backend takes `ShiftAssignmentRequest`, whose three
 * target fields are all nullable — it assigns to whichever one is set.
 */
export type ShiftAssignmentInput = {
  shiftId: number;
  employeeId?: number;
  departmentId?: number;
  branchId?: number;
};

/** Every shift in the company. No per-branch scoping — shifts are global. */
export function getShifts(signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<ShiftResponse[]> {
  return api.get<ShiftResponse[]>("/shifts", { signal });
}

/** Creates a shift. SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN, HR_MANAGER. */
export function createShift(payload: ShiftInput): Promise<ShiftResponse> {
  return api.post<ShiftResponse>("/shifts", payload);
}

/** Renames or retimes a shift. Same roles as create. */
export function updateShift(
  id: number,
  payload: ShiftInput,
): Promise<ShiftResponse> {
  return api.put<ShiftResponse>(`/shifts/${id}`, payload);
}

/** Assigns a shift to an employee, a department or a branch. */
export function assignShift(
  payload: ShiftAssignmentInput,
): Promise<ShiftAssignmentResponse> {
  return api.post<ShiftAssignmentResponse>("/shifts/assignments", payload);
}

/** One employee's shift assignments. */
export function getShiftAssignmentsForEmployee(
  employeeId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<ShiftAssignmentResponse[]> {
  return api.get<ShiftAssignmentResponse[]>(
    `/shifts/assignments/employee/${employeeId}`,
    { signal },
  );
}

/** Everything rostered in one branch — the roster screen's source. */
export function getShiftAssignmentsForBranch(
  branchId: number,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<ShiftAssignmentResponse[]> {
  return api.get<ShiftAssignmentResponse[]>(
    `/shifts/assignments/branch/${branchId}`,
    { signal },
  );
}

===== src/lib/api/user.ts =====
import "server-only";

import { api } from "@/app/lib/api-client";
import type { UserSummaryResponse } from "@/types";

/**
 * `controller/auth/UserController` — base `/users`.
 *
 * The list and the deactivate are two different audiences, which is the whole
 * reason this module documents them separately:
 *
 *   GET  /users              → SUPER_ADMIN, ADMIN
 *   PUT  /users/{id}/deactivate → SUPER_ADMIN only
 *   POST /users              → SUPER_ADMIN only
 *
 * So an ADMIN may read the directory but may not deactivate or create — the
 * UI hides those controls for ADMIN rather than rendering buttons that 403.
 * That is a deliberate frontend rule mirroring `@PreAuthorize`, not a backend
 * restriction being worked around.
 *
 * `GET /users` returns a bare `List<UserSummaryResponse>`, not a page: there is
 * no paging on this endpoint, so a paged table here would be theatre.
 */

/** Every account. SUPER_ADMIN and ADMIN. */
export function getUsers(signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<UserSummaryResponse[]> {
  return api.get<UserSummaryResponse[]>("/users", { signal });
}

/**
 * Deactivates an account. SUPER_ADMIN only.
 *
 * There is no matching reactivate endpoint — reactivation is not currently a
 * product capability, so nothing here pretends otherwise.
 */
export function deactivateUser(userId: number): Promise<void> {
  return api.put<void>(`/users/${userId}/deactivate`);
}

===== src/types/index.ts =====
/**
 * Domain types mirroring `V1__baseline_schema.sql` and the backend
 * `dto/response` package.
 *
 * Conventions:
 *  - `id` is BIGSERIAL server-side → `number` here.
 *  - NUMERIC(15,2) money columns → `number`. Format at the edge, never compute
 *    in the browser; the payroll engine is authoritative.
 *  - DATE → "yyyy-MM-dd", TIMESTAMP → ISO-8601, TIME → "HH:mm:ss".
 */

import type { Role } from "@/config/roles";

export type { Role, Permission, PrincipalType } from "@/config/roles";

/* --------------------------------------------------------------- common -- */

/** BaseEntity columns present on every table. */
export type AuditFields = {
  createdAt: string | null;
  updatedAt: string | null;
  createdBy: string | null;
  updatedBy: string | null;
};

/**
 * Backend `dto/response/PageResponse` — a trimmed projection of Spring Data
 * `Page<T>`, not the raw Jackson serialization. There is no `first` flag;
 * derive it from `pageNumber === 0`.
 */
export type Paginated<T> = {
  content: T[];
  pageNumber: number;
  pageSize: number;
  totalElements: number;
  totalPages: number;
  last: boolean;
};

/** Return shape for Server Actions wired to `useActionState`. */
export type ActionState<T = void> =
  | { status: "idle" }
  | { status: "success"; data: T }
  | {
      status: "error";
      message: string;
      fieldErrors?: Record<string, string[] | undefined>;
    };

/* ---------------------------------------------------------- enumerations -- */

/**
 * `branch.status` and `agent.status` — a plain `String` column on both entities,
 * defaulting to "ACTIVE".
 *
 * There is no SUSPENDED. Deactivation writes "INACTIVE" and nothing writes
 * anything else (`BranchServiceImpl.deactivateBranch:73`,
 * `AgentServiceImpl.deactivateAgent:68`), so a third member would only ever
 * describe a state the backend cannot produce.
 */
export const STATUSES = ["ACTIVE", "INACTIVE"] as const;
export type Status = (typeof STATUSES)[number];

// Mirrors EmploymentType in the backend entity package:
// PERMANENT, CONTRACT, CASUAL, OUTSOURCED. There is no INTERN.
export const EMPLOYMENT_TYPES = [
  "PERMANENT",
  "CONTRACT",
  "CASUAL",
  "OUTSOURCED",
] as const;
export type EmploymentType = (typeof EMPLOYMENT_TYPES)[number];

// Employee.employmentStatus is a plain String in the backend, defaulted to
// "ACTIVE" and documented there as ACTIVE, INACTIVE, TERMINATED — that comment
// is the only definition of the domain, and terminateEmployee() sets
// "TERMINATED". PROBATION / ON_LEAVE / SUSPENDED do not exist anywhere in the
// backend; ON_LEAVE is an *attendance* status, not an employment one.
export const EMPLOYMENT_STATUSES = [
  "ACTIVE",
  "INACTIVE",
  "TERMINATED",
] as const;
export type EmploymentStatus = (typeof EMPLOYMENT_STATUSES)[number];

export const GENDERS = ["MALE", "FEMALE", "OTHER"] as const;
export type Gender = (typeof GENDERS)[number];

export const MARITAL_STATUSES = [
  "SINGLE",
  "MARRIED",
  "DIVORCED",
  "WIDOWED",
] as const;
export type MaritalStatus = (typeof MARITAL_STATUSES)[number];

export const DOCUMENT_TYPES = [
  "CONTRACT",
  "NATIONAL_ID",
  "KRA_PIN",
  "CERTIFICATE",
  "CV",
  "OTHER",
] as const;
export type DocumentType = (typeof DOCUMENT_TYPES)[number];

/**
 * `agents.charge_type` — mirrors `entity/agent/ChargeType.java` exactly.
 *
 * The previous list (`FIXED` / `PERCENTAGE`) named values the backend enum does
 * not have, so a form built from it would have POSTed a charge type the server
 * rejects. `AgentRequest.chargeType` is typed as the Java enum, so this is the
 * set that actually round-trips.
 */
export const CHARGE_TYPES = [
  "PER_EMPLOYEE",
  "PERCENTAGE_OF_GROSS",
  "FIXED_MONTHLY",
  "CUSTOM",
] as const;
export type ChargeType = (typeof CHARGE_TYPES)[number];

export const ADVANCE_STATUSES = [
  "PENDING",
  "APPROVED",
  "REJECTED",
  "OUTSTANDING",
  "RECOVERED",
] as const;
export type AdvanceStatus = (typeof ADVANCE_STATUSES)[number];

/** `attendance.source` — MANUAL entries are auditable, BIOMETRIC are not editable. */
export const ATTENDANCE_SOURCES = ["BIOMETRIC", "MANUAL", "MOBILE", "WEB"] as const;
export type AttendanceSource = (typeof ATTENDANCE_SOURCES)[number];

export const ATTENDANCE_STATUSES = [
  "PRESENT",
  "ABSENT",
  "LATE",
  "HALF_DAY",
  "ON_LEAVE",
  "HOLIDAY",
] as const;
export type AttendanceStatus = (typeof ATTENDANCE_STATUSES)[number];

export const DEVICE_STATUSES = ["ONLINE", "OFFLINE", "DISABLED"] as const;
export type DeviceStatus = (typeof DEVICE_STATUSES)[number];

/**
 * `entity/shift/ShiftType`.
 *
 * These are shift *types*, not shift names — the seeded demo shifts are called
 * "Day", "Night" and "Split", and their types are MORNING, NIGHT and EVENING.
 * A form that offers the names as the type sends values the Java enum rejects.
 */
export const SHIFT_TYPES = [
  "MORNING",
  "EVENING",
  "NIGHT",
  "ROTATING",
  "FLEXIBLE",
] as const;
export type ShiftType = (typeof SHIFT_TYPES)[number];

/**
 * Two-stage approval: `approved_by_manager_id` then `approved_by_hr_id`.
 * PENDING_HR means the branch manager has signed off and HR is next.
 *
 * `entity/leave/LeaveStatus` — the values are exactly these four. There is no
 * plain PENDING and no CANCELLED.
 */
export const LEAVE_STATUSES = [
  "PENDING_MANAGER",
  "PENDING_HR",
  "APPROVED",
  "REJECTED",
] as const;
export type LeaveStatus = (typeof LEAVE_STATUSES)[number];

export const LOAN_STATUSES = [
  "PENDING",
  "APPROVED",
  "REJECTED",
  "ACTIVE",
  "SETTLED",
] as const;
export type LoanStatus = (typeof LOAN_STATUSES)[number];

export const PAYROLL_RUN_STATUSES = [
  "DRAFT",
  "PROCESSING",
  "COMPLETED",
  "APPROVED",
  "FAILED",
  "CANCELLED",
] as const;
export type PayrollRunStatus = (typeof PAYROLL_RUN_STATUSES)[number];

export const PAYROLL_PERIODS = ["MONTHLY", "BIWEEKLY", "WEEKLY"] as const;
export type PayrollPeriod = (typeof PAYROLL_PERIODS)[number];

export const NOTIFICATION_CHANNELS = ["IN_APP", "EMAIL", "SMS"] as const;
export type NotificationChannel = (typeof NOTIFICATION_CHANNELS)[number];

/* ------------------------------------------------- attendance dashboard -- */

/**
 * Read projections from `controller/attendance/AttendanceDashboardController`.
 *
 * These are NOT the `Attendance` entity above. That type mirrors the
 * `attendance` table; these mirror what the dashboard actually computes — an
 * employee who is still on shift is `IN_PROGRESS`, one who has clocked out is
 * `COMPLETED`, and a record's `hoursWorked` is a decimal number of hours. The
 * `ATTENDANCE_STATUSES` union above deliberately does not cover them: widening
 * it would let an entity-shaped row carry a projection status, and every
 * existing consumer would silently accept it.
 */

/** Which way the finger went. Decided on the terminal, not by the server. */
export const ATTENDANCE_ACTIVITY_TYPES = ["CLOCK_IN", "CLOCK_OUT"] as const;
export type AttendanceActivityType = (typeof ATTENDANCE_ACTIVITY_TYPES)[number];

export const ATTENDANCE_RECORD_STATUSES = ["IN_PROGRESS", "COMPLETED"] as const;
export type AttendanceRecordStatus = (typeof ATTENDANCE_RECORD_STATUSES)[number];

/** A device that has never reported. Distinct from OFFLINE, which has. */
export const BRANCH_DEVICE_STATUSES = ["ONLINE", "OFFLINE", "NO_DEVICE"] as const;
export type BranchDeviceStatus = (typeof BRANCH_DEVICE_STATUSES)[number];

export type AttendanceDashboardSummary = {
  /** The business-day the figures cover, in the company timezone. */
  date: string;
  generatedAt: string;
  expectedEmployees: number;
  reported: number;
  currentlyIn: number;
  clockedOut: number;
  notYetIn: number;
  attendanceRatePercent: number;
  devicesTotal: number;
  devicesOnline: number;
  branches: AttendanceBranchSummary[];
};

export type AttendanceBranchSummary = {
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string;
  branchCode: string | null;
  expected: number;
  reported: number;
  currentlyIn: number;
  clockedOut: number;
  notYetIn: number;
  attendanceRatePercent: number;
  lastActivityAt: string | null;
  deviceStatus: BranchDeviceStatus;
  deviceLastSyncAt: string | null;
};

/** One row of the live feed. A single attendance record yields up to two. */
export type AttendanceActivityItem = {
  attendanceId: number;
  employeeId: number;
  employeeName: string | null;
  employeeNumber: string | null;
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string | null;
  type: AttendanceActivityType;
  occurredAt: string;
  source: AttendanceSource;
};

export type AttendanceRecordItem = {
  id: number;
  employeeId: number;
  employeeName: string | null;
  employeeNumber: string | null;
  position: string | null;
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string | null;
  attendanceDate: string;
  clockIn: string | null;
  clockOut: string | null;
  /** Decimal hours. Not minutes — see `formatHours` in `lib/format`. */
  hoursWorked: number | null;
  status: AttendanceRecordStatus;
  source: AttendanceSource;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/attendance/AttendanceResponse` — what the plain attendance
 * endpoints return.
 *
 * Distinct from `AttendanceRecordItem` above, which is the *dashboard
 * controller's* projection. That one adds `employeeNumber`, `position`,
 * `branchName` and `hoursWorked`; this one has none of them. Rendering a record
 * from `/attendance/employee/{id}` through the dashboard type would show four
 * columns that are always null.
 *
 * `status` here is the raw `attendance.status` column, not the dashboard's
 * derived `AttendanceRecordStatus`. The Java DTO declares it as a plain
 * `String`, and the entity's own comment lists OVERTIME among the values while
 * `ATTENDANCE_STATUSES` does not — so a row can legitimately carry a value
 * outside the union below. Consumers must handle an unrecognised status rather
 * than assume the union is exhaustive.
 */
export type AttendanceResponse = {
  id: number;
  employeeId: number;
  employeeName: string | null;
  branchId: number;
  attendanceDate: string;
  clockIn: string | null;
  clockOut: string | null;
  source: AttendanceSource;
  status: AttendanceStatus;
};

export type AttendanceDeviceItem = {
  id: number;
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string | null;
  deviceSerial: string;
  status: string;
  lastSyncAt: string | null;
  /** Reported inside the backend's 5-minute freshness window. */
  online: boolean;
};

/**
 * A scan the backend refused — unmapped finger, wrong branch, employee on
 * leave, inactive employee. Kept on the dashboard because it is the only place
 * an administrator finds out that a clock-in silently did not happen.
 */
export type AttendanceSyncFailure = {
  id: number;
  createdAt: string;
  eventId: string;
  deviceSerial: string | null;
  employeeId: number | null;
  employeeName: string | null;
  reason: string;
};

/** Payload of the polling route at `/api/attendance/live`. */
export type AttendanceLiveSnapshot = {
  summary: AttendanceDashboardSummary;
  activity: AttendanceActivityItem[];
  fetchedAt: string;
};

/* ------------------------------------------------- wire response types -- */

/**
 * The shapes the backend actually returns, named after the Java records in
 * `dto/response/**`.
 *
 * These are deliberately separate from the entity-shaped types above. The
 * entities carry `AuditFields` and every column; the response records are
 * projections that drop most of them and add a few computed ones. Treating the
 * two as the same type is how a page ends up rendering `createdAt` as
 * `undefined` and calling a column "missing" when it was never sent.
 *
 * Where a record omits a field the entity has, that is intentional — those
 * widgets have no data source and must render an honest empty state rather
 * than a plausible-looking zero.
 */

/** `dto/response/company/CompanyResponse`. Note: no audit fields, no counts. */
export type CompanyResponse = {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  logoUrl: string | null;
  address: string | null;
  phone: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  website: string | null;
  kraPin: string | null;
  shifNumber: string | null;
  nssfNumber: string | null;
  nitaNumber: string | null;
  currency: string | null;
  timezone: string | null;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/company/CompanySettingsResponse`.
 *
 * `companyId` is absent: the deployment is single-tenant and the backend
 * resolves the company itself, so the client never needs to send one.
 */
export type CompanySettingsResponse = {
  id: number;
  vatRate: number;
  defaultWorkingHoursPerDay: number | null;
  overtimeMultiplier: number | null;
  weekendDays: string | null;
  payrollPeriod: PayrollPeriod | null;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/branch/BranchResponse`.
 *
 * No region, headcount, attendance rate or payroll total — `branches` has no
 * region column at all and the rest are aggregates the backend does not
 * compute. The branch list must not show those columns.
 */
export type BranchResponse = {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  code: string;
  address: string | null;
  phone: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  managerId: number | null;
  managerName: string | null;
  status: Status;
};

/** `dto/response/department/DepartmentResponse`. */
export type DepartmentResponse = {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string | null;
  headOfDepartmentId: number | null;
  headOfDepartmentName: string | null;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/agent/AgentResponse`.
 *
 * `employeeCount` is the one aggregate the backend does supply, so the agent
 * list can show placements as a real number.
 */
export type AgentResponse = {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  contactPerson: string | null;
  phone: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  address: string | null;
  bankName: string | null;
  bankAccountNumber: string | null;
  kraPin: string | null;
  contractStartDate: string | null;
  contractEndDate: string | null;
  chargeType: ChargeType;
  chargeAmount: number | null;
  chargePercentage: number | null;
  vatRegistered: boolean;
  status: Status;
  employeeCount: number | null;
};

/** `dto/response/employee/EmployeeSummaryResponse` — what the by-* lists return. */
export type EmployeeSummaryResponse = {
  id: number;
  fullName: string;
  employeeNumber: string;
  branchName: string | null;
  position: string | null;
  employmentStatus: EmploymentStatus;
};

/** `dto/response/employee/EmployeeResponse` — the full record. */
export type EmployeeResponse = {
  id: number;
  fullName: string;
  employeeNumber: string;
  passportPhotoUrl: string | null;
  nationalId: string | null;
  dateOfBirth: string | null;
  gender: string | null;
  maritalStatus: string | null;
  phone: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  address: string | null;
  kraPin: string | null;
  shifNumber: string | null;
  nssfNumber: string | null;
  nitaNumber: string | null;
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string | null;
  departmentId: number | null;
  departmentName: string | null;
  agentId: number | null;
  agentName: string | null;
  position: string | null;
  employmentType: EmploymentType;
  basicSalary: number | null;
  dateHired: string | null;
  employmentStatus: EmploymentStatus;
  bankName: string | null;
  bankAccountNumber: string | null;
  emergencyContactName: string | null;
  emergencyContactPhone: string | null;
};

/** `dto/response/employee/EmployeeDocumentResponse`. */
export type EmployeeDocumentResponse = {
  id: number;
  employeeId: number;
  documentType: string;
  fileUrl: string;
  fileName: string | null;
  uploadedAt: string | null;
};

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ shift -- */

/** `dto/response/shift/ShiftResponse`. Times are `HH:mm:ss`, or null. */
export type ShiftResponse = {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  type: ShiftType;
  startTime: string | null;
  endTime: string | null;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/shift/ShiftAssignmentResponse`.
 *
 * A shift is assigned to exactly one of an employee, a department or a branch —
 * `employeeId`, `departmentId` and `branchId` are three nullable columns, and
 * only one is normally set. `shiftName` is the only joined field.
 */
export type ShiftAssignmentResponse = {
  id: number;
  shiftId: number;
  shiftName: string;
  employeeId: number | null;
  departmentId: number | null;
  branchId: number | null;
};

/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ leave -- */

/** `dto/response/leave/LeaveTypeResponse`. */
export type LeaveTypeResponse = {
  id: number;
  name: string;
  defaultEntitlementDays: number | null;
  paid: boolean;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/leave/LeaveRequestResponse`.
 *
 * `status` is a plain String in the backend and is typed as the `LeaveStatus`
 * union here. There is no `days` field — a screen wanting the duration has to
 * compute it from `startDate` and `endDate`.
 */
export type LeaveRequestResponse = {
  id: number;
  employeeId: number;
  employeeName: string | null;
  leaveTypeId: number;
  leaveTypeName: string | null;
  startDate: string;
  endDate: string;
  reason: string | null;
  status: LeaveStatus;
  rejectionReason: string | null;
};

/** `dto/response/leave/LeaveBalanceResponse`. */
export type LeaveBalanceResponse = {
  leaveTypeId: number;
  leaveTypeName: string;
  entitledDays: number;
  usedDays: number;
  remainingDays: number;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/attendance/BiometricDeviceResponse`.
 *
 * Only reachable per branch (`GET /attendance/devices/by-branch/{id}`). For the
 * whole fleet use `AttendanceDeviceItem` from the dashboard controller, which
 * also carries the server-computed `online` flag.
 */
export type BiometricDeviceResponse = {
  id: number;
  branchId: number;
  branchName: string | null;
  deviceSerial: string;
  lastSyncAt: string | null;
  status: Status;
};

/**
 * `dto/response/auth/UserSummaryResponse` — `GET /users`.
 *
 * Deliberately thin, and the thinness matters: there is **no** last-login and
 * **no** MFA field, because `users` has no `last_login_at` and no `mfa` column.
 * A user directory that shows either would be inventing it.
 *
 * `branchId` rather than a branch name — the row carries the foreign key only,
 * so a table showing the branch must resolve it against `GET /branches`.
 *
 * The backend declares `role` as a plain `String` (the column is a single
 * VARCHAR), while the frontend narrows to the `Role` union. The union is the
 * tighter of the two and matches `Role.java`, so it is used here; an
 * unrecognised value would be a backend contract change, not a client case.
 */
export type UserSummaryResponse = {
  id: number;
  username: string;
  role: Role;
  displayName: string | null;
  branchId: number | null;
  active: boolean;
};

# This is NOT the Next.js you know

This version has breaking changes — APIs, conventions, and file structure may all differ from your training data. Read the relevant guide in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` (resolved from this file's directory; in monorepos the `next` package may not be visible from the repo root) before writing any code. This block is written and re-added by `next dev` — verify at `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files.js`. Removing it from a diff only re-creates the uncommitted change; committing it with your work keeps the tree clean.
